   770	    async def _search_with_multi_expansion(
   771	        self,
   772	        query: str,
   773	        user_level: int,
   774	        limit: int = 5,
   775	        tier_filter: list[TierLevel] | None = None,
   776	        apply_filters: bool | None = None,
   777	    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
   778	        """
   779	        Search with LLM-generated multi-query expansion and RRF fusion.
   780	
   781	        Generates 2-3 alternative queries via Gemini Flash, searches each,
   782	        then fuses results using Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF).
   783	
   784	        Only activated when ENABLE_QUERY_EXPANSION=true and query > 4 words.
   785	
   786	        Args:
   787	            query: Original user query
   788	            user_level: User access level (0-3)
   789	            limit: Max final results after fusion
   790	            tier_filter: Optional tier restriction
   791	            apply_filters: Whether to apply filters
   792	
   793	        Returns:
   794	            Search results dict with fused results, or empty if expansion fails.
   795	        """
   796	        from backend.services.search.query_expander import get_query_expander
   797	
   798	        expander = get_query_expander()
   799	        alt_queries = await expander.expand_multi(query)
   800	
   801	        if len(alt_queries) <= 1:
   802	            # No expansion happened — fall through to normal search
   803	            return {}
   804	
   805	        logger.info("Multi-query expansion: '%s' → %d queries", query[:40], len(alt_queries))
   806	
   807	        # Search each alternative (limit=3 per query to keep cost/latency down)
   808	        per_query_limit = max(3, limit)
   809	        all_results: list[list[dict[str, Any]]] = []
   810	
   811	        async def _single_search(q: str) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   812	            try:
   813	                async with asyncio.timeout(8.0):
   814	                    res = await self._single_query_search(
   815	                        query=q,
   816	                        user_level=user_level,
   817	                        limit=per_query_limit,
   818	                        tier_filter=tier_filter,
   819	                        apply_filters=apply_filters,
   820	                    )
   821	                    return res.get("results", [])
   822	            except Exception as e:
   823	                logger.warning("Multi-expansion sub-query failed: %s", e)
   824	                return []
   825	
   826	        # Structured concurrency (Python 3.11+); _single_search swallows exceptions.
   827	        async with asyncio.TaskGroup() as tg:
   828	            sub_tasks = [tg.create_task(_single_search(q)) for q in alt_queries]
   829	        sub_results = [t.result() for t in sub_tasks]
   830	        for sr in sub_results:
   831	            if sr:
   832	                all_results.append(sr)
   833	
   834	        if not all_results:
   835	            return {}
   836	
   837	        # RRF fusion across result sets
   838	        fused = self._rrf_fuse_multi(all_results, k=60)
   839	
   840	        # Deduplicate by document ID
   841	        seen_ids: set[str] = set()
   842	        deduped: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   843	        for doc in fused:
   844	            doc_id = doc.get("id", "")
   845	            if doc_id and doc_id not in seen_ids:
   846	                seen_ids.add(doc_id)
   847	                deduped.append(doc)
   848	            elif not doc_id:
   849	                # No ID — dedup by first 100 chars of text
   850	                text_key = doc.get("text", "")[:100]
   851	                if text_key not in seen_ids:
   852	                    seen_ids.add(text_key)
   853	                    deduped.append(doc)
   854	
   855	        final_results = deduped[:limit]
   856	
   857	        return {
   858	            "query": query,
   859	            "results": final_results,
   860	            "user_level": user_level,
   861	            "allowed_tiers": [],
   862	            "collection_used": "multi_expansion",
   863	            "expansion_queries": len(alt_queries),
   864	            "expansion_applied": True,
   865	        }
   866	
   867	    async def _single_query_search(
   868	        self,
   869	        query: str,
   870	        user_level: int,
   871	        limit: int = 5,
   872	        tier_filter: list[TierLevel] | None = None,
   873	        apply_filters: bool | None = None,
   874	    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
   875	        """Execute a single search query (no expansion, no recursion)."""
   876	        embedding_start = time.time() if METRICS_AVAILABLE else None
   877	        (
   878	            query_embedding,
   879	            collection_name,
   880	            vector_db,
   881	            chroma_filter,
   882	            _tier_values,
   883	        ) = await self._prepare_search_context(query, user_level, tier_filter, None, apply_filters)
   884	        if METRICS_AVAILABLE and embedding_start:
   885	            rag_embedding_duration.observe(time.time() - embedding_start)
   886	
   887	        # Determine vector name
   888	        use_vector_name = "dense" if _uses_named_vectors(collection_name) else None
   889	
   890	        search_start = time.time() if METRICS_AVAILABLE else None
   891	        raw_results = await vector_db.search(
   892	            query_embedding=query_embedding,
   893	            filter=chroma_filter,
   894	            limit=limit,
   895	            vector_name=use_vector_name,
   896	        )
   897	        if METRICS_AVAILABLE and search_start:
   898	            rag_vector_search_duration.observe(time.time() - search_start)
   899	
   900	        formatted_results = format_search_results(
   901	            raw_results,
   902	            collection_name,
   903	            primary_collection=None,
   904	            query=query,
   905	            # B1.1: raw_results above came from the dense path, so the kind is
   906	            # known here and is declared rather than left to the UNKNOWN default.
   907	            score_kind=score_provenance.DENSE_FORMATTED,
   908	        )
   909	        return {
   910	            "query": query,
   911	            "results": formatted_results,
   912	            "collection_used": collection_name,
   913	        }
   914	
   915	    @staticmethod
   916	    def _rrf_fuse_multi(
   917	        result_sets: list[list[dict[str, Any]]],
   918	        k: int = 60,
   919	    ) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   920	        """
   921	        Reciprocal Rank Fusion across multiple result sets.
   922	
   923	        RRF score = sum(1/(k + rank_i)) for each result set containing the doc.
   924	        """
   925	        scores: dict[str, float] = {}
   926	        docs: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
   927	
   928	        for result_set in result_sets:
   929	            for rank, doc in enumerate(result_set, start=1):
   930	                doc_id = doc.get("id", doc.get("text", "")[:100])

  1085	
  1086	        Results are cached in Redis (TTL 1h) keyed by query+user_level+limit+collection.
  1087	        Cache reduces avg latency from ~894ms to <100ms for repeated queries.
  1088

  1090	            query: Search query
  1091	            user_level: User access level (0-3)

  1097	                error in the hybrid path falls back to `self.search()` —
  1098	                which runs LLM query expansion (Gemini, via QueryExpander).
  1099	                Pass False on paths with a zero-LLM contract (the WA
  1100	                codex-route package builder, BOT-V4 S2): the error is
  1101	                re-raised so the caller degrades WITHOUT an LLM ever being

  1128	                ):

  1436	
  1437	                # Determine allowed tiers (only for zantara_books)
  1438	                allowed_tiers = self.LEVEL_TO_TIERS.get(user_level, [])
  1439	                if tier_filter:
  1440	                    allowed_tiers = [t for t in allowed_tiers if t in tier_filter]
  1441	
  1442	                # Build filter (only for zantara_books)
  1443	                tier_filter_dict = None
  1444	                if collection_name == "zantara_books" and allowed_tiers:
  1445	                    tier_values = [t.value for t in allowed_tiers]
  1446	                    tier_filter_dict = {"tier": {"$in": tier_values}}
  1447	
  1448	                # Build combined filter with default exclusion of repealed laws
  1449	                chroma_filter = build_search_filter(
  1450	                    tier_filter=tier_filter_dict,
  1451	                    exclude_repealed=True,
  1452	                    exclude_historical=self._requires_current_law_guard(collection_name),
  1453	                )
  1454	
  1455	                # Search this collection (async) - track duration
  1456	                use_vector_name = "dense" if _uses_named_vectors(collection_name) else None
  1457	                search_start = time.time() if METRICS_AVAILABLE else None
  1458	                raw_results = await vector_db.search(
  1459	                    query_embedding=query_embedding,
  1460	                    filter=chroma_filter,
  1461	                    limit=limit,
  1462	                    vector_name=use_vector_name,
  1463	                )
  1464	                if METRICS_AVAILABLE and search_start:
  1465	                    rag_vector_search_duration.observe(time.time() - search_start)
  1466	
  1467	                # Format results using helper method
  1468	                formatted_results = format_search_results(
  1469	                    raw_results,
  1470	                    collection_name,
  1471	                    primary_collection=primary_collection,
  1472	                    query=query,
  1473	                    # B1.1: raw_results above came from the dense path, so the kind is
  1474	                    # known here and is declared rather than left to the UNKNOWN default.
  1475	                    score_kind=score_provenance.DENSE_FORMATTED,
  1476	                )
  1477	
  1478	                return collection_name, formatted_results
  1479	
  1480	            # Execute all searches in parallel (track parallel execution)
  1481	            if METRICS_AVAILABLE:
  1482	                rag_parallel_searches.inc(len(collections_to_search))
  1483	            search_tasks = [search_single_collection(coll) for coll in collections_to_search]
  1484	            search_results = await asyncio.gather(*search_tasks, return_exceptions=True)
  1485	
  1486	            # Process results and collect health metrics for batch recording
  1487	            results_by_collection = {}
  1488	            health_metrics = []
  1489	
  1490	            for result in search_results:
  1491	                if isinstance(result, Exception):
  1492	                    logger.error("Search task failed: %s", result)
  1493	                    continue
  1494	                collection_name, formatted_results = result
  1495	
  1496	                if formatted_results:
  1497	                    results_by_collection[collection_name] = formatted_results
  1498	                    logger.info(
  1570	            # Fallback to simple search
  1571	            return await self.search(query, user_level, limit, tier_filter)
  1572	
  1573	    def get_conflict_stats(self) -> dict:
  1574	        """Get comprehensive conflict resolution statistics.
  1575	
  1576	        Aggregates metrics from ConflictResolver and SearchService to provide
  1577	        insights into multi-collection search behavior and conflict patterns.
  1578	
  1579	        REFACTORED: Delegates to ConflictResolver for core metrics.
  1580	
  1581	        Returns:
  1582	            Dict with conflict resolution metrics:
  1583	                - total_multi_collection_searches (int): Total multi-collection queries
  1584	                - conflicts_detected (int): Number of conflicts found
  1585	                - conflicts_resolved (int): Number successfully resolved
  1586	                - timestamp_resolutions (int): Conflicts resolved by timestamp
  1587	                - conflict_rate (str): Percentage of searches with conflicts
  1588	                - resolution_rate (str): Percentage of conflicts successfully resolved
  1589	
  1590	        Note:
  1591	            - Includes both detection and resolution metrics
  1592	            - Rates calculated as percentages with 1 decimal place
  1593	            - Useful for monitoring data quality and consistency
  1594	
  1595	        Example:
  1596	            >>> stats = search_service.get_conflict_stats()
  1597	            >>> logger.info(f"Conflict rate: {stats['conflict_rate']}")
  1598	            >>> logger.info(f"Resolution rate: {stats['resolution_rate']}")
  1599	        """
  1600	        resolver_stats = self.conflict_resolver.get_stats()
  1601	        total_searches = self.conflict_stats["total_multi_collection_searches"]
  1602	        conflict_rate = (
  1603	            (resolver_stats["conflicts_detected"] / total_searches * 100)
  1604	            if total_searches > 0
  1605	            else 0.0
  1606	        )
  1607	
  1608	        return {
  1609	            **self.conflict_stats,
  1610	            **resolver_stats,
  1611	            "conflict_rate": f"{conflict_rate:.1f}%",
  1612	            "resolution_rate": f"{(resolver_stats['conflicts_resolved'] / resolver_stats['conflicts_detected'] * 100) if resolver_stats['conflicts_detected'] > 0 else 0:.1f}%",
  1613	        }
  1614	
  1615	    async def search_collection(
  1616	        self,
  1617	        query: str,
  1618	        collection_name: str,
  1619	        limit: int = 5,
  1620	        filter: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
  1621	    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
  1622	        """
  1623	        Direct search on a specific collection (e.g., for few-shot examples).
  1624	
  1625	        Args:
  1626	            query: Search query
  1627	            collection_name: Target collection
  1628	            limit: Max results
  1629	            filter: Optional filter
  1630	
  1631	        Returns:
  1632	            Search results
  1633	        """
  1634	        try:
  1635	            # Generate embedding
  1636	            query_embedding = await self.embedder.generate_query_embedding(query)
  1637	
  1638	            # Get client (lazy loading)
  1639	            client = self.collection_manager.get_collection(collection_name)
  1640	            if not client:
  1641	                # Create ad-hoc client for new collections like conversation_examples
  1642	                from backend.core.qdrant_db import QdrantClient
  1643	
  1644	                client = QdrantClient(
  1645	                    qdrant_url=settings.qdrant_url,
  1646	                    collection_name=collection_name,
  1647	                )
  1648	
  1649	            # Search (async)
  1650	            raw_results = await client.search(
  1651	                query_embedding=query_embedding,
  1652	                filter=filter,
  1653	                limit=limit,
  1654	            )
  1655	
  1656	            # Format results using helper method
  1657	            formatted_results = format_search_results(
  1658	                raw_results,
  1659	                collection_name,
  1660	                primary_collection=None,
  1661	                query=query,
  1662	                # B1.1: raw_results above came from the dense path, so the kind is
  1663	                # known here and is declared rather than left to the UNKNOWN default.
  1664	                score_kind=score_provenance.DENSE_FORMATTED,
  1665	            )
  1666	
  1667	            return {"query": query, "results": formatted_results, "collection": collection_name}
  1668	
  1669	        except (qdrant_exceptions.UnexpectedResponse, httpx.HTTPError, ValueError, KeyError) as e:
  1670	            logger.error("Collection search failed: %s", e, exc_info=True)
  1671	            return {"results": [], "error": str(e)}

   473	# `_canonical_wire`/`_package_hash` in a throwaway checkout, on the inputs
   474	# below, and pasting the results here as literals. They are the pre-PR
   475	# oracle. Computing them with the CURRENT code instead would make the test
   476	# tautological: a coherent change to BOTH the serializer and the digest
   477	# would break every stored package while keeping the assertion green
   478	# (Codex round 1, finding 8 — that is exactly what the first version did).
   479	_BASE_SEALED_WIRE = (

   481	    '"text":"legacy chunk, no score_kind"}],'
   482	    '"evidence_inputs":{"abstain":false,"context_length":1,"dlp":false,'

   484	    '"history":[{"content":"hi","role":"user"}],"persona_digest":"pd",'
   485	    '"pricing_block":null,"thread_epoch":1}'
   486	)
   487	_BASE_SEALED_DIGEST = "466643afc3c393d49fcaddd4a92f5f8be63d64e88dc351c6c90921edc84a4454"
   488

   490	_BASE_SEALED_CHUNKS = [
   491	    {"collection": "visa_oracle", "text": "legacy chunk, no score_kind", "score": 0.7}
   492	]
   493	_BASE_SEALED_EVIDENCE_INPUTS = {
   494	    "evidence_score": 0.5,

   503	def test_a_package_sealed_before_score_kind_existed_still_verifies() -> None:
   504	    """A package sealed by the BASE code — chunks with no score_kind/score_raw
   505	    key at all — must still verify, and emit the same bytes, under this PR.
   506	
   507	    `ContextPackage.__post_init__` (wa_package_builder.py :153-172) recomputes
   508	    the wire and raises ValueError unless the digest matches, so constructing
   509	    against the FROZEN base digest without raising is the verification the
   510	    deploy boundary performs when it reloads a stored package.
   511	    """
   512	    assert score_provenance.SCORE_KIND_KEY not in _BASE_SEALED_CHUNKS[0]
   513	    assert score_provenance.SCORE_RAW_KEY not in _BASE_SEALED_CHUNKS[0]
   514	
   515	    package = ContextPackage(
   516	        history=_BASE_SEALED_HISTORY,
   517	        chunks=_BASE_SEALED_CHUNKS,
   518	        pricing_block=None,
   519	        persona_digest="pd",
   520	        evidence_inputs=_BASE_SEALED_EVIDENCE_INPUTS,
   521	        thread_epoch=1,
   522	        package_hash=_BASE_SEALED_DIGEST,
   523	    )
   524	    assert package.package_hash == _BASE_SEALED_DIGEST
   525	    # Not just "it verifies": the bytes this PR puts on the wire for a
   526	    # pre-PR package are byte-identical to the ones the base emitted.
   527	    assert package.wire_text() == _BASE_SEALED_WIRE

   530	def test_the_frozen_base_digest_rejects_altered_bytes() -> None:
   531	    """The companion to the test above, and the reason it means anything: the
   532	    frozen digest must FAIL on content it does not cover. Without this, a
   533	    __post_init__ that verified nothing would pass the compatibility test.
   534	    """
   535	    altered_chunks = [dict(_BASE_SEALED_CHUNKS[0], score=0.71)]
   536	    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="package_hash does not cover"):
   537	        ContextPackage(

   545	        )

   915	    @staticmethod
   916	    def _rrf_fuse_multi(
   917	        result_sets: list[list[dict[str, Any]]],
   918	        k: int = 60,
   919	    ) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   920	        """
   921	        Reciprocal Rank Fusion across multiple result sets.
   922	
   923	        RRF score = sum(1/(k + rank_i)) for each result set containing the doc.
   924	        """
   925	        scores: dict[str, float] = {}
   926	        docs: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
   927	
   928	        for result_set in result_sets:
   929	            for rank, doc in enumerate(result_set, start=1):
   930	                doc_id = doc.get("id", doc.get("text", "")[:100])
   931	                if doc_id not in docs:
   932	                    docs[doc_id] = doc
   933	                    scores[doc_id] = 0.0
   934	                scores[doc_id] += 1.0 / (k + rank)
   935	
   936	        # Sort by RRF score descending
   937	        sorted_ids = sorted(scores.keys(), key=lambda x: scores[x], reverse=True)
   938	
   939	        result = []
   940	        for doc_id in sorted_ids:
   941	            doc = docs[doc_id].copy()
   942	            doc["rrf_score"] = round(scores[doc_id], 6)
   943	            result.append(doc)
   944	
   945	        return result

   947	    def _init_reranker(self) -> Any:
   948	        """Lazy load the re-ranker based on config.
   949	
   950	        Checks ``settings.reranker_backend`` (env ``RERANKER_BACKEND``):
   951	          - ``"cross-encoder"`` -> local CrossEncoderReranker (free, ~75MB model)
   952	          - ``"zerank2"``       -> external Ze-Rank 2 API (requires API key)
   953	
   954	        When ``reranker_backend`` is ``"cross-encoder"``, the reranker is
   955	        explicitly enabled regardless of the legacy ``enable_reranker`` flag.
   956	        """
   957	        if not hasattr(self, "_reranker"):
   958	            backend: str = getattr(settings, "reranker_backend", "cross-encoder")
   959	            # HONESTY FIX (2026-07-18): this used to hardcode enabled=True
   960	            # regardless of settings.enable_reranker (default False — the
   961	            # cross-encoder model is intentionally NOT bundled in the prod
   962	            # image, saves ~5GB). That forced CrossEncoderReranker to try
   963	            # loading sentence_transformers on every call, fail with
   964	            # "No module named 'sentence_transformers'", and silently
   965	            # return zero scores — while callers still believed reranking
  1300	            logger.info(
  1301	                f"⚡ Early exit: Top result score {results['results'][0]['score']:.3f} > 0.85",
  1302	            )

  1309	
  1310	        # 3. Re-rank with Ze-Rank 2
  1311	        reranker = self._init_reranker()
  1312	        if reranker.enabled:
  1313	            logger.info(
  1314	                f"🔍 Re-ranking {len(results['results'])} hybrid candidates for: '{query[:50]}'",
  1315	            )
  1316	            rerank_start = time.time() if METRICS_AVAILABLE else None
  1317	            reranked_docs = await reranker.rerank(query, results["results"], top_k=limit)
  1318	            if METRICS_AVAILABLE and rerank_start:
  1319	                rag_reranking_duration.observe(time.time() - rerank_start)
  1320	            results["results"] = reranked_docs
  1321	            results["reranked"] = True
  1322	            results["early_exit"] = False
  1323	        else:
  1324	            results["results"] = results["results"][:limit]
  1325	            results["reranked"] = False
  1326	            results["early_exit"] = False
  1327	
  1328	        # Track total pipeline duration
  1329	        if METRICS_AVAILABLE and pipeline_start_time:
  1330	            rag_pipeline_duration.observe(time.time() - pipeline_start_time)
  1331	
  1332	        reranker_label: str = getattr(settings, "reranker_backend", "cross-encoder")
  1333	        results["pipeline"] = f"hybrid_bm25_rrf_{reranker_label}"
  1334	        return results
  1335	
  1336	    @cached(ttl=300, prefix="rag_multi_search")
  1337	    async def search_with_conflict_resolution(
  1338	        self,
  1339	        query: str,
  1340	        user_level: int,
  1341	        limit: int = 5,
  1342	        tier_filter: list[TierLevel] = None,
  1343	        enable_fallbacks: bool = True,
  1344	    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
  1345	        """Enhanced multi-collection search with conflict detection and resolution.
  1346	
  1347	        Implements the Supreme Knowledge Architecture's conflict resolution pipeline:
  1348	        1. Intelligent routing: Classify query and determine primary collection
  1349	        2. Fallback chains: Add secondary collections based on confidence
  1350	        3. Parallel search: Query all relevant collections concurrently
  1351	        4. Conflict detection: Identify contradicting or outdated information
  1352	        5. Resolution: Apply timestamp-based and semantic deduplication
  1353	        6. Merge & rank: Combine results with score-based ordering
  1354	
  1355	        Conflict Detection Algorithm:
    70	_MAX_HISTORY_CONTENT_CHARS = 4096
    71	
    72	# pricing_block per-field allowlist (S2 cross-family review, finding 2):
    73	# PricingService.search_service() returns contact_info (WhatsApp number,
    74	# wa.me link), disclaimers and raw catalogue entries with internal
    75	# annotations (_sub_block). Only these fields may cross into the package —
    76	# "no phone" is part of the spec's allowlist contract.
    77	_PRICING_ENTRY_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    78	    "key",
    79	    "name",
    80	    "price",
    81	    "tier_range",
    82	    "duration",
    83	    "validity",
    84	    "notes",
    85	)
    86	
    87	# Fixed, documented access level for this deterministic, unauthenticated WA
    88	# path — matches `VectorSearchTool.__init__`'s own default (tools.py:89),
    89	# the closest existing analog for "no elevated tier, anonymous caller".
    90	_SEARCH_USER_LEVEL = 1
    91	
    92	# Persona digest: a MINIMAL, deterministic set of the existing SSOT prompt
    93	# sections from backend/prompts/zantara_core.py (the sole prompt SSOT per
    94	# apps/backend-rag/CLAUDE.md "Prompt Architecture") — chosen because they
    95	# most directly constrain HOW a WA reply gets written (language selection,
    96	# citation discipline, greeting handling). Deliberately excluded: the
    97	# creator/team personas, the master template, and the internal-monologue /
    98	# escalation / crash-protocol sections — those are orchestration-only
    99	# (deciding what tools to call, how to escalate) and irrelevant to a
   100	# downstream text generator that receives an already-built evidence
   101	# package and just has to write the reply.
   102	_PERSONA_SECTIONS: tuple[str, ...] = (LANGUAGE_PROTOCOL, CITATION_RULES, GREETING_RULES)
   103	
   104	
   105	class PackageUnbuildable(Exception):
   106	    """The deterministic pipeline cannot build a package for this query.
   107	
   108	    The caller (the wa_package router) treats this as a ROUTE decision, not
   109	    an error: the whole row goes to the Gemini leg, never an LLM-borrowed
   110	    fallback inside the codex path (spec §2.2).
   111	    """
   112	
   113	    def __init__(self, reason: str) -> None:
   114	        self.reason = reason
   115	        super().__init__(reason)
   116	
   117	
   118	@dataclass(frozen=True)
   119	class ContextPackage:
   120	    """The deterministic context package for the codex-route WA generation leg.
   121	
   122	    Allowlist schema (spec §2.2) — EXACTLY these 7 fields serialize via
   123	    `to_payload()`. Adding an 8th field to this dataclass does NOT
   124	    automatically leak it into the wire payload: `to_payload()` builds the
   125	    dict literally (never `dataclasses.asdict()` plus extras), so the
   126	    allowlist is enforced by construction, not by convention.
   127	    """
   128	
   129	    history: list[dict[str, str]]
   130	    chunks: list[dict[str, Any]]
   131	    pricing_block: dict[str, Any] | None
   132	    persona_digest: str
   133	    evidence_inputs: dict[str, Any]
   134	    thread_epoch: int
   135	    package_hash: str
   136	    # DLP reversal map (G-P3), placeholder -> original PII value. Populated
   137	    # ONLY when `build_context_package(dlp=True)` redacted this package's
   138	    # history/chunks; empty otherwise. Deliberately EXCLUDED from
   139	    # `_canonical_wire`/`__post_init__`'s hash domain, from `to_payload()`
   140	    # and from `wire_text()` — it is not part of the 7-key allowlist and
   141	    # must never enter the wire package, the package_hash inputs, or a log
   142	    # line (spec: "never leaves Fly"). `repr=False` so an accidental
   143	    # `logger.debug(package)`/`repr(package)` cannot print original PII.
   144	    reversal_map: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict, repr=False, compare=False)
   145	    # Wire snapshot, sealed at construction (Codex S2 re-verdict r6,
   146	    # finding 2): `frozen=True` freezes the field BINDINGS only — the
   147	    # nested lists/dicts stay mutable, so serializing them lazily would let
   148	    # `pkg.history[-1]["content"] = ...` between build and offer divorce
   149	    # the wire bytes from package_hash. Serializing ONCE here, and
   150	    # verifying the hash against the snapshot in __post_init__, makes the
   151	    # pair immutable together for the object's whole life.
   152	    _wire: str = field(init=False, repr=False, compare=False, default="")
   153	
   154	    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
   155	        wire = _canonical_wire(
   156	            {
   157	                "history": self.history,
   158	                "chunks": self.chunks,
   159	                "pricing_block": self.pricing_block,
   160	                "persona_digest": self.persona_digest,
   161	                "evidence_inputs": self.evidence_inputs,
   162	                "thread_epoch": self.thread_epoch,
   163	            }
   164	        )
   165	        digest = hashlib.sha256(wire.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
   166	        if digest != self.package_hash:
   167	            raise ValueError(
   168	                "package_hash does not cover this package's wire bytes "
   169	                "(hash/wire integrity is sealed at construction)"
   170	            )
   171	        object.__setattr__(self, "_wire", wire)
   172	
   173	    def to_payload(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
   174	        """Serialize to the exact 7-key allowlist. No extras, ever.
   175	
   176	        Returns a DEEP COPY (S2 cross-family review, finding 5):
   177	        `frozen=True` freezes the field BINDINGS, not the mutable
   178	        lists/dicts inside them — handing out the internal references
   179	        would let a caller mutate content after `package_hash` was
   180	        computed, silently divorcing the payload from its own hash.
   181	
   182	        NOT the broker wire format: this 7-key view INCLUDES package_hash,
   183	        so its serialization can never equal the bytes the hash covers —
   184	        offering `json.dumps(to_payload())` as broker_jobs.package makes
   185	        every broker-side hash verification fail by construction (Codex S2
   186	        re-verdict r5, finding 1). The envelope the broker receives and
   187	        verifies is ``wire_text()``; the hash travels beside it.
   188	        """
   189	        return copy.deepcopy(
   190	            {
   191	                "history": self.history,
   192	                "chunks": self.chunks,
   193	                "pricing_block": self.pricing_block,
   194	                "persona_digest": self.persona_digest,
   195	                "evidence_inputs": self.evidence_inputs,
   196	                "thread_epoch": self.thread_epoch,
   197	                "package_hash": self.package_hash,
   198	            }
   199	        )
   200	
   201	    def wire_text(self) -> str:
   202	        """The EXACT bytes offered to the broker as broker_jobs.package —
   203	        and, verbatim, the bytes ``package_hash`` covers.
   204	
   205	        One serializer (``_canonical_wire``) produces both this string and
   385	            for service_name, entry in items.items():
   386	                kept_entry = _filter_entry(entry)
   387	                if kept_entry:
   388	                    kept_services[str(service_name)] = kept_entry
   389	            if kept_services:
   390	                clean_results[str(category)] = kept_services
   391	                continue
   392	        dropped_categories += 1
   393	    if dropped_categories:
   394	        logger.info(
   395	            "wa_package_builder: dropped %d pricing categor(ies) with no allowlisted entries",
   396	            dropped_categories,
   397	        )
   398	    if not clean_results:
   399	        return None
   400	    return {"search_query": str(raw.get("search_query", "")), "results": clean_results}
   401	
   402	
   403	def _cap_chunks(chunks: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   404	    """Enforce the chunk-count and per-chunk-length hygiene caps, LOGGING every drop."""
   405	    capped: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   406	    truncated_count = 0
   407	    for chunk in chunks:
   408	        text = chunk["text"]
   409	        if len(text) > _MAX_CHUNK_CHARS:
   410	            text = text[:_MAX_CHUNK_CHARS]
   411	            truncated_count += 1
   412	        capped.append({**chunk, "text": text})
   413	
   414	    dropped_count = max(0, len(capped) - _MAX_CHUNKS)
   415	    if dropped_count:
   416	        logger.info(
   417	            "wa_package_builder: dropping %d/%d chunks over the %d-chunk cap",
   418	            dropped_count,
   419	            len(capped),
   420	            _MAX_CHUNKS,
   421	        )
   422	    if truncated_count:
   423	        logger.info(
   424	            "wa_package_builder: truncated %d chunk(s) over the %d-char cap",
   425	            truncated_count,
   426	            _MAX_CHUNK_CHARS,
   427	        )
   428	    return capped[:_MAX_CHUNKS]
   429	
   430	
   431	def _canonical_wire(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
   432	    """The ONE serializer for the broker envelope — used by BOTH
   433	    ``ContextPackage.wire_text()`` and ``_package_hash``, so the bytes on
   434	    the wire and the bytes under the digest are the same function's output
   435	    by construction (Codex S2 re-verdict r5, finding 1). `sort_keys=True`
   436	    makes it independent of dict insertion order; compact `separators` plus
   437	    `ensure_ascii=False` keep it deterministic for identical content."""
   438	    return json.dumps(payload, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False, separators=(",", ":"))
   439	
   440	
   441	def _package_hash(
   442	    history: list[dict[str, str]],
   443	    chunks: list[dict[str, Any]],
   444	    pricing_block: dict[str, Any] | None,
   445	    persona_digest: str,
   446	    evidence_inputs: dict[str, Any],
   447	    thread_epoch: int,
   448	) -> str:
   449	    """Content-addressed sha256 hex digest over the 6 non-hash fields —
   450	    digested over exactly ``_canonical_wire`` of them, i.e. exactly the
   451	    string ``wire_text()`` later emits."""
   452	    payload = {
   453	        "history": history,
   454	        "chunks": chunks,
   455	        "pricing_block": pricing_block,
   456	        "persona_digest": persona_digest,
   457	        "evidence_inputs": evidence_inputs,
   458	        "thread_epoch": thread_epoch,
   459	    }
   460	    return hashlib.sha256(_canonical_wire(payload).encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
   461	
   462	
   463	async def build_context_package(
   464	    *,
   465	    query: str,
   550	    retrieved.sort(key=lambda c: (-float(c["score"]), c["collection"], c["text"]))
   551	    chunks.extend(retrieved)
   552	
   553	    chunks = _cap_chunks(chunks)
   554	
   555	    pricing_block: dict[str, Any] | None = None
   556	    if has_pricing_intent(query):
   557	        pricing_block = _sanitize_pricing_block(get_pricing_service().search_service(query))
   558	
   559	    payload_history = _sanitize_history(history, query)
   560	
   561	    # G-P3 DLP gate — runs AFTER chunks are capped and history sanitized,
   562	    # BEFORE evidence_inputs/package_hash are computed: everything hashed
   563	    # and sealed below is the REDACTED content, never the original (spec
   564	    # rule: "package_hash computed over the REDACTED content"). Fail-closed
   565	    # by construction — ANY exception from the redact step (detector bug,
   566	    # or wa_dlp's own >MAX_PLACEHOLDERS overflow guard) becomes
   567	    # PackageUnbuildable so the row falls off to the Gemini leg instead of
   568	    # shipping a partially-redacted or unredacted package.
   569	    #
   570	    # M1 (Kimi adversarial review, verified): `pricing_block["search_query"]`
   571	    # is the customer's RAW query, verbatim (pricing_service.py sets it from
   572	    # the same `query` argument `_sanitize_pricing_block` keeps untouched) —
   573	    # without threading it through the SAME redaction state as history, a
   574	    # NIK in the query would be redacted in `history[-1]["content"]` but
   575	    # ship unredacted a few keys over in `pricing_block.search_query`, into
   576	    # both the wire and the hash. Passed into `redact_package_fields` so it
   577	    # shares dedup/placeholder-numbering with the history copy of the same
   578	    # string, then spliced back before hashing.
   579	    reversal_map: dict[str, str] = {}
   580	    if dlp:
   581	        pricing_search_query = (
   582	            pricing_block.get("search_query") if pricing_block is not None else None
   583	        )
   584	        try:
   585	            dlp_result = redact_package_fields(payload_history, chunks, pricing_search_query)
   586	        except Exception as exc:
   587	            logger.info(
   588	                "wa_package_builder: dlp redaction failed error=%s",
   589	                type(exc).__name__,
   590	            )
   591	            raise PackageUnbuildable(reason="dlp_error") from exc
   592	        payload_history = dlp_result.history
   593	        chunks = dlp_result.chunks
   594	        reversal_map = dlp_result.reversal_map
   595	        if pricing_block is not None:
   596	            pricing_block = {**pricing_block, "search_query": dlp_result.search_query}
   597	
   598	    persona_digest = _build_persona_digest()
   599	
   600	    # Evidence inputs are FROZEN here — finalization (spec §2.3) reads these
   601	    # fields, it never recomputes them against a possibly-drifted retrieval.
   602	    abstain_policy = build_abstain_policy(query)
   603	    evidence_score = calculate_evidence_score(
   604	        sources=[
   605	            {
   606	                "score": chunk["score"],
   607	                "score_kind": score_provenance.kind_of(chunk),
   608	                "score_raw": score_provenance.raw_of(chunk),
   609	            }
   610	            for chunk in chunks
   611	        ],
   612	        context_gathered=[chunk["text"] for chunk in chunks],
   613	        query=query,
   614	    )
   615	    evidence_inputs: dict[str, Any] = {
   616	        "evidence_score": evidence_score,
   617	        "context_length": len(chunks),
   618	        "domain": plan.domain.value,
   619	        "label_threshold": abstain_policy.label_threshold,
   620	        "abstain": abstain_policy.label_abstains(evidence_score),
   621	        # Kimi-7 (MINOR): inside the hash domain by design — a future
   622	        # caller that forgets `dlp=True` produces a package distinguishable
   623	        # downstream (evidence_inputs is what finalize/consumers read out
   624	        # of the sealed wire) instead of one indistinguishable from a
   625	        # properly-redacted build.
   626	        "dlp": dlp,
   627	    }
   628	
   629	    package_hash = _package_hash(
   630	        history=payload_history,
   631	        chunks=chunks,
   632	        pricing_block=pricing_block,
   633	        persona_digest=persona_digest,
   634	        evidence_inputs=evidence_inputs,
   635	        thread_epoch=thread_epoch,
   636	    )
   637	
   638	    return ContextPackage(
   639	        history=payload_history,
   640	        chunks=chunks,
   641	        pricing_block=pricing_block,
   642	        persona_digest=persona_digest,
   643	        evidence_inputs=evidence_inputs,
   644	        thread_epoch=thread_epoch,
   645	        package_hash=package_hash,
   646	        reversal_map=reversal_map,
   647	    )
   190	# worst case with these still in the vocabulary: "Apa itu PT PMA?" against a
   191	# chunk that only names an unrelated company ("PT Warung Bali menjual nasi
   192	# goreng ...") went 0.080 -> 0.800 (with sources) — abstain became an answer
   193	# from an unrelated chunk. See
   194	# test_non_discriminating_two_character_tokens_were_removed_from_the_vocabulary.
   195	_SHORT_IDENTIFIERS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
   196	    {
   197	        # Company / licensing
   198	        "pma", "nib", "oss", "tdp",
   199	        # `sk` (Surat Keputusan / decision letter) stays: measured, unlike
   200	        # `pt`/`cv` it is not a common standalone word in ordinary Indonesian
   201	        # or English prose, so it is not a systemic free hit.
   202	        "sk",
   203	        # Tax
   204	        "pph", "ppn", "spt", "pbb", "pkp",
   205	        # Immigration — the permit codes a client actually types
   206	        "voa", "imk", "tka", "wna", "wni",
   207	        "b1", "c1", "c2", "c7", "d1", "d2", "e23", "e28", "e31", "e32", "e33",
   208	        # Property
   209	        "hgb", "shm", "imb", "pbg", "slf", "ajb", "hak",
   210	    }
   211	)

   213	
   214	def calculate_evidence_score(
   215	    sources: list[dict] | None,
   216	    context_gathered: list[str],
   217	    query: str,
   218	) -> float:
   219	    """
   220	    Calculate evidence score based on source quality and context relevance.
   221	
   222	    FIXED: This score now properly reflects ACTUAL relevance between query and context.
   223	    - Mismatched results (e.g., KITAS query returning KBLI) will score < 0.15
   224	    - Nonsense queries ("xyzabc123") will score ~0.0
   225	    - Relevant results with good sources score 0.6+
   226	
   227	    Scoring Formula:
   228	        - Base score: 0.0
   229	        - Source quality component (0.0-0.4): Based on top source score
   230	        - Context relevance (0.0-0.6): Based on keyword/semantic overlap
   231	        - Maximum score: 1.0
   232	
   233	    Args:
   234	        sources: List of source dictionaries with 'score' field
   235	        context_gathered: List of context strings from tool results
   236	        query: Original user query string
   237	
   238	    Returns:
   239	        Evidence score between 0.0 and 1.0
   240	
   241	    Note:
   242	        - Score < 0.15: ABSTAIN (insufficient evidence)
   243	        - Score 0.15-0.6: CAUTIOUS (low confidence, use fallback)
   244	        - Score > 0.6: CONFIDENT (proceed with answer)
   245	    """
   246	    if not sources and not context_gathered:
   247	        return 0.0
   248	
   249	    # Extract meaningful query keywords (remove stop words, short words)
   250	    query_lower = query.lower()
   610	        source_scores = [s.get("score", 0.0) for s in sources if isinstance(s, dict)]
   611	
   612	        if source_scores:
   613	            top_score = max(source_scores)
   614	
   615	            # Source quality based on top score
   616	            # - Score 0.7+ : 0.4 points (excellent)
   617	            # - Score 0.5-0.7: 0.3 points (good)
   618	            # - Score 0.3-0.5: 0.2 points (acceptable)
   619	            # - Score < 0.3: 0.0-0.1 points (weak)
   620	            if top_score >= 0.7:
   621	                source_quality_score = 0.4
   622	            elif top_score >= 0.5:
   623	                source_quality_score = 0.3
   624	            elif top_score >= 0.3:
   625	                source_quality_score = 0.2
   626	            elif top_score >= 0.15:
   627	                source_quality_score = 0.1
   628	            else:
   629	                source_quality_score = 0.0
   630	
   631	    # ========== FINAL SCORE CALCULATION ==========
   632	    # Semantic relevance is the PRIMARY factor (60-80% weight)
   633	    # Source quality acts as a bonus/boost (20-40% weight)
   634	
   635	    if semantic_relevance == 0.0:
   636	        # No semantic relevance - score is based only on weak source quality
   637	        # This ensures nonsense queries or complete mismatches score < 0.15
   638	        final_score = min(source_quality_score * 0.2, 0.1)
   639	    elif semantic_relevance < 0.3:
   640	        # Weak relevance - cap the score regardless of source quality
   641	        # Max possible: 0.2 + (0.4 * 0.25) = 0.3
   642	        final_score = semantic_relevance + source_quality_score * 0.25
   643	        final_score = min(final_score, 0.35)
   644	    else:
   645	        # Good relevance - can add source quality as bonus
   646	        # But cap at 1.0 and weight source quality lower
   647	        final_score = semantic_relevance + source_quality_score * 0.5
   648	
   649	    # ========== SEMANTIC ALIGNMENT PENALTY (v3 quick-win #1) ==========
   650	    # Use Qdrant's per-source cosine similarity as a sanity gate. If retrieval
   651	    # returned sources but the BEST one has cosine < 0.5 with the query, the
   652	    # keyword-overlap path was probably misled (e.g. KITAS query → KBLI docs
   653	    # share the word "business" but are semantically unrelated). Penalize
   654	    # final score by 0.7× — does not create false negatives because a truly
   655	    # weak retrieval already had low source_quality_score; this just prevents
   656	    # the keyword-driven semantic_relevance from inflating the verdict.
   657	    #
   658	    # Source `score` is set by Qdrant (cosine similarity for our hybrid
   659	    # collections). Skip when sources are absent or top_score is missing.
   660	    if sources:
   661	        top_source_cosine = max(
   662	            (s.get("score", 0.0) for s in sources if isinstance(s, dict)),
   663	            default=0.0,
   664	        )
   665	        if 0 < top_source_cosine < 0.5 and final_score > 0.15:
   666	            penalized = round(final_score * 0.7, 2)
   667	            logger.info(
   668	                "evidence_score: semantic-alignment penalty applied (top_cosine=%.2f, %.2f → %.2f)",
   669	                top_source_cosine,
   670	                final_score,
   671	                penalized,
   672	            )
   673	            final_score = penalized
   674	
   675	    return round(min(final_score, 1.0), 2)
   676	
   677	
   678	# ============================================================================
   679	# Domain-aware ABSTAIN thresholds (v3 quick-win #2)
   680	# ============================================================================
   681	# A single global ABSTAIN_THRESHOLD (0.15) over-rejects on Indonesian-language
   682	# domains (tax, visa) where docs naturally have lower keyword overlap with
   683	# IT/EN queries, and under-rejects on KBLI where false-positive risk is
   684	# higher (many business-vocabulary collisions). Per-domain thresholds let
   685	# the gate breathe in the right direction.
   686	#
   687	# The ENV override DOMAIN_ABSTAIN_THRESHOLDS=tax:0.10,kbli:0.20,default:0.15
   688	# is read at process start and merged on top of these defaults.
   689	
   690	DOMAIN_ABSTAIN_THRESHOLDS_DEFAULT: dict[str, float] = {
   691	    "tax": 0.10,  # Indonesian tax docs, lower keyword overlap with IT/EN
   692	    "visa": 0.12,  # immigration docs are mostly Indonesian/English mix
   693	    "pricing": 0.15,  # baseline
   694	    "kbli": 0.20,  # higher bar — business vocabulary creates false positives
   695	    "default": 0.15,
   696	}
   697	
   698	
   699	def _parse_domain_threshold_overrides(spec: str) -> dict[str, float]:
   700	    """Parse `DOMAIN_ABSTAIN_THRESHOLDS=tax:0.10,kbli:0.20` env var format."""
   701	    out: dict[str, float] = {}
   702	    for raw in (spec or "").split(","):
   703	        raw = raw.strip()
   704	        if not raw or ":" not in raw:
   705	            continue
   706	        key, _, val = raw.partition(":")
   707	        try:
   708	            value = float(val)
   709	        except ValueError:
   710	            logger.warning(
   711	                "DOMAIN_ABSTAIN_THRESHOLDS: skipping malformed entry %r",
   712	                raw,
   713	            )
   714	            continue
   715	        # Evidence scores live in [0.0, 1.0]; an override outside that range
   716	        # (including nan/inf, which fail this comparison) would silently
   717	        # disable or force the abstain gate for a whole domain — skip it.
   718	        if not (0.0 <= value <= 1.0):
   719	            logger.warning(
   720	                "DOMAIN_ABSTAIN_THRESHOLDS: skipping out-of-range entry %r",
   721	                raw,
   722	            )
   723	            continue
   724	        out[key.strip().lower()] = value
   725	    return out
   726	
   727	
   728	def _build_domain_thresholds() -> dict[str, float]:
   729	    merged = dict(DOMAIN_ABSTAIN_THRESHOLDS_DEFAULT)
   730	    merged.update(
   731	        _parse_domain_threshold_overrides(
   732	            os.environ.get("DOMAIN_ABSTAIN_THRESHOLDS", ""),
   733	        ),
   734	    )
   735	    return merged
   736	
   737	
   738	_DOMAIN_THRESHOLDS = _build_domain_thresholds()
   739	
   740	
   741	def classify_query_domain(query: str) -> str:
   742	    """Lightweight domain classifier for ABSTAIN-threshold lookup.
   743	
   744	    Returns one of: tax, visa, pricing, kbli, default.
   745	
   746	    Intentionally a tiny heuristic — full intent classification happens
   747	    elsewhere; this is just the routing key for per-domain ABSTAIN tuning.
   748	    """
   749	    q = (query or "").lower()
   750	    if any(
   751	        kw in q
   752	        for kw in (
   753	            "tax",
   754	            "tasse",
   755	            "imposta",
   756	            "pajak",
   757	            "ppn",
   758	            "pph",
   759	            "npwp",
   760	            "spt",
   761	            "pkp",
   762	            "fiscal",
   763	            "fiscale",
   764	            "tarif pajak",
   765	        )
   766	    ):
   767	        return "tax"
   768	    if any(
   769	        kw in q
   770	        for kw in (
   771	            "visa",
   772	            "visto",
   773	            "kitas",
   774	            "kitap",
   775	            "imigrasi",
   776	            "immigration",
   777	            "stay permit",
   778	            "permesso di soggiorno",
   779	            "rptka",
   780	            "itk",
   781	            "c1",
   782	            "c2",
   783	            "d1",
   784	            "d2",
   785	            "e33g",
   786	            "b211",
   787	        )
   788	    ):
   789	        return "visa"
   790	    if any(
   791	        kw in q
   792	        for kw in (
   793	            "kbli",
   794	            "codice kbli",
   795	            "kode kbli",
   796	            "klasifikasi",
   797	            "classification",
   798	            "kegiatan usaha",
   799	            "business activity",
   800	        )
   801	    ):
   802	        return "kbli"
   803	    if any(
   804	        kw in q
   805	        for kw in (
   806	            "quanto costa",
   807	            "price",
   808	            "prezzo",
   809	            "costo",
   810	            "harga",
   811	            "berapa biaya",
   812	            "cost",
   813	            "pricing",
   814	            "tariffa",
   815	            "fee",
   816	        )
   817	    ):
   818	        return "pricing"
   819	    return "default"
   820

@pytest.fixture
def mock_db_pool():
    """Standard mock asyncpg connection pool.

    Supports: async with pool.acquire() as conn
              async with conn.transaction()  (no-op async context manager)
    Usage: pool, conn = mock_db_pool
    """
    pool = MagicMock()
    conn = MagicMock()

    class _AsyncCtx:
        async def __aenter__(self):
            return conn

        async def __aexit__(self, *args):
            return None

    class _TransactionCtx:
        async def __aenter__(self):
            return None

        async def __aexit__(self, *args):
            return None

    conn.fetchrow = AsyncMock(return_value=None)
    conn.fetchval = AsyncMock(return_value=None)
    conn.fetch = AsyncMock(return_value=[])
    conn.execute = AsyncMock(return_value=None)
    pool.acquire = MagicMock(return_value=_AsyncCtx())
    # conn.transaction() must return an async context manager, not a coroutine.
    # AsyncMock would make it a coroutine; use MagicMock returning _TransactionCtx.
    conn.transaction = MagicMock(return_value=_TransactionCtx())
    return pool, conn


@pytest.fixture
def mock_qdrant_client():
    """Standard mock Qdrant client."""
    client = AsyncMock()
    client.search = AsyncMock(return_value=[])
    client.upsert = AsyncMock(return_value=None)
    client.delete = AsyncMock(return_value=None)
    client.get_collections = AsyncMock(return_value=MagicMock(collections=[]))
    return client


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def _wr2_runtime_isolation(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """Tests must NEVER touch the real WR2 runtime state (W96, 2026-07-13).

    Any test that reaches wr2_html_render_apply's visibility chain (or any
    other WR2 writer honoring WR2_OUTPUT_ROOT) without mocking it would land
    fixture entries in the PRODUCTION human-review-queue.json and spool real
    Telegram notifications — 24 phantom "micro carousels" reached the WR2
    Control app this way. Redirect both to tmp_path unconditionally; a test
    that needs its own root still wins by monkeypatching the env itself
    (test-body setenv runs after this autouse fixture).

    Same reasoning, added 2026-08-20: claude_vision.py's chain-wide rate-limit
    cooldown + no-op fingerprint cache default to ~/.agent/state/*.json — an
    unmocked test that trips a "rate-limited" path would otherwise write a
    REAL cooldown file that then blocks the PRODUCTION vision loop for up to
    WR2_VISION_COOLDOWN_S (default 1h), discovered live when the existing
    _run_claude_json test battery (rate-limit/timeout/rotation cases) started
    failing each other in file order via the shared default path.
    """
    monkeypatch.setenv("WR2_OUTPUT_ROOT", str(tmp_path / "wr2-output"))
    monkeypatch.setenv("TG_DRY_RUN", "1")
    monkeypatch.setenv("TG_SPOOL_DIR", str(tmp_path / "tg-spool"))
    monkeypatch.setenv("WR2_VISION_COOLDOWN_STATE", str(tmp_path / "wr2-vision-cooldown.json"))
    monkeypatch.setenv(
        "WR2_VISION_FINGERPRINT_CACHE", str(tmp_path / "wr2-vision-fingerprints.json")
    )

## Critical Gotchas

### Sentry PII Redaction Is Load-Bearing (2026-04-21)

`backend/app/setup/sentry_config.py::_before_send` is the only thing stopping
UU PDP-regulated PII (NPWP, NIB, passport, email, phone, client_id, name,
surname) from leaking to Sentry cloud on every exception. Three rules:

1. **Never remove the `before_send=_before_send` kwarg** from `sentry_sdk.init`.
   Sentry has NO opt-in knob for this behavior — without the hook, every
   unhandled exception with a request body in locals leaks PII.
2. **Never set `SENTRY_SEND_DEFAULT_PII=1`** in production. `send_default_pii`
   would add IP addresses and user headers on top of the fields the hook is
   already redacting; `scripts/sentry-quota-check.sh` alerts on this.
3. **Never raise `SENTRY_TRACES_SAMPLE_RATE > 0.02`** in production. Free tier
   is 5k events/month shared across errors AND transactions; APM sampling
   burns it silently, after which error events are dropped too. Default is
   `0.0` in prod — APM is opt-in per deploy.
4. **Never remove `include_local_variables=False`** from `sentry_sdk.init`
   (added 2026-08-29, gate F3 of PR #4959). `_before_send`/`_scrub` only ever
   see the JSON-shaped event payload — but the SDK's logging integration
   attaches RAW frame-local values to `stacktrace.frames[].vars` at capture
   time, before `before_send` runs, whenever this is left at the SDK default
   of `True` (the pre-2.x SDK called this kwarg `with_locals`; measure the
   installed `sentry_sdk` version before assuming the name). A field that
   exists only as a bare local — never a dict key — bypasses key-based
   redaction entirely regardless of how complete `_PII_KEY_SUBSTRINGS` is;
   see `garuda_portal_auth.py`'s `exchange_magic_link` handler for the
   concrete case (`garuda_result_session` / `result_session_secret`) that
   forced this. Test: `test_init_sentry_disables_local_variable_capture` in
   `tests/unit/app/setup/test_sentry_pii_redaction.py`.

When you add a new PII-bearing field to a router:

- Add the token to `_PII_KEY_SUBSTRINGS` in `sentry_config.py` (or to
  `_PII_EXACT_KEYS` if the bare key would collide with debug fields —
  e.g. `name` is exact-match-only because `filename`/`function_name`
  must survive).
- Add a case to `PII_SAMPLES` in `tests/test_sentry_pii_redaction.py`.
- Run `SKIP_SENTRY_INIT=1 PYTHONPATH=. pytest tests/test_sentry_pii_redaction.py -q`.

Kill-switch for the whole integration: `fly secrets set SKIP_SENTRY_INIT=1 -a nuzantara-rag`.

### Migration Runner Was Executing ROLLBACK Section In-Transaction (SCAR 2026-04-19, FIXED)

`backend/db/migration_base.py` `BaseMigration.apply()` previously read the
**full** content of every `.sql` file in `db/migrations_v2/`, including the
section after the `-- === ROLLBACK ===` marker, and passed it all to
`conn.execute(sql)`. PostgreSQL treats `-- === ROLLBACK ===` as a normal
comment, so CREATE TABLE followed by DROP TABLE ran in the same transaction:
the migration logged "applied successfully" and then the table vanished. The
next migration found "relation does not exist" and failed.

Concrete symptom from PB2 deploy 2026-04-19:

```
Migration 114_compliance_alerts applied successfully in 67ms   ← log says OK
Applying migration 115_alert_outcomes
ERROR: SQL execution failed: relation "compliance_alerts" does not exist
```

**Fix (branch `fix/migration-runner-rollback-strip`):**
- Added `ROLLBACK_MARKER_RE` regex to `migration_base.py`.
- New `split_migration_sql(sql) -> (forward, rollback)` helper.
- `BaseMigration.apply()` now uses only the forward portion.
- Checksum still computed on the full file content for audit stability.
- 2 integration tests added that drive real `apply()` and
  `apply_all_pending()` against PG (the previous tests validated only the
  extraction helper, not the apply path).

**Cost of incident:** 19-commit revert of `pro/backend-compliance-intel-e2e`
from main + ~2-3 hours debug. PB2 branch preserved on origin, ready to be
rebased on the fix branch and redeployed.

**Diagnostic note for future Claude:** an earlier draft of this SCAR (now
overwritten) attributed the failure to "cross-connection visibility on Fly
ephemeral machine". That hypothesis was wrong. The bug was reproducible
locally as soon as you exercised the real `BaseMigration.apply()` path
(not the extraction helper test). Lesson: **reproduce the bug on the
real call path locally before theorizing**, especially for failures observed
only in deploy logs.

**Convention going forward:** any new SQL migration in `db/migrations_v2/`
SHOULD include a `-- === ROLLBACK ===` section. The runner now correctly
extracts the forward DDL only.

### Docker Build Context — Monorepo Root (PR #62)

The Dockerfile expects build context at **monorepo root**, not `apps/backend-rag/`.
Fly uses this via `flyctl deploy --dockerfile apps/backend-rag/Dockerfile --config apps/backend-rag/fly.toml` in `.github/workflows/fly-deploy.yml`.

**Local `docker build` — RUN FROM REPO ROOT:**

```bash
cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"
docker build -f apps/backend-rag/Dockerfile -t rag-test .
```

**Do NOT** `cd apps/backend-rag && docker build -t rag-test .` — that excludes `packages/cell-core/` from context and breaks the cell-core editable install.

Authoritative `.dockerignore` is at repo root (`/.dockerignore`); `apps/backend-rag/.dockerignore` is retained but IGNORED by Fly.

### SQLite Persistence — `/data` Volume on `api` Process (PR #75)

The `api` process has a persistent Fly volume (`nuzantara_api_data → /data`). Two SQLite databases live there:

- `EXPERIENCE_DB_PATH=/data/experience.db` — Genome (Experience/Skill)
- `METABOLIC_DB_PATH=/data/organism_metrics.db` — Metabolic snapshots

Both env vars are set in `fly.toml [env]`. Data survives rolling deploys. The `rag` process has its own separate volume (`nuzantara_rag_data`).

### Router Registration — Manifest + Parity Gate (PR #63, corrected 2026-06-13)

> CORRECTION 2026-06-13: this section previously claimed `router_registration.py`
> "reads from router_manifest.py" and "no other file needs editing" — FALSE.
> Registration is EXPLICIT imports + `include_router` calls (see scar #424:
> the fix WAS editing registration). The drift the old text declared
> "structurally impossible" happened repeatedly (#54/#55/#60, #422→#424,
> olympus/intel trio caught 2026-06-13). What IS structural now is the
> parity test below.

To add a new router (all 3 steps, same PR):

1. Create file in `backend/app/routers/`
2. Add `RouterEntry` in `backend/app/setup/router_manifest.py` with correct `process_groups`
3. Add the matching `api.include_router(...)` calls in `backend/app/setup/router_registration.py`:
   ALWAYS in `include_routers()` (monolithic = full union), PLUS
   `include_light_routers()` for `_API` and/or `include_heavy_routers()` for `_RAG`

Then run BOTH gates:

```bash
PYTHONPATH=. pytest backend/tests/setup/test_router_manifest.py \
  backend/tests/setup/test_manifest_registration_parity.py -q
```

Process groups: `_API` (light, public HTTP via main_api), `_RAG` (heavy, internal via main_rag), `_BOTH` (health checks).

**SCAR:** PRs #54/#55/#60 registered routers only in `include_routers()` (dev) but not `include_light_routers()` (prod) → silent 404s; #422 needed hotfix #424 for the same class. `test_manifest_registration_parity.py` (2026-06-13) enforces bidirectional manifest ↔ registration parity per process group — on its first run it caught `olympus.internal_router` missing from main_api prod and the `intel` trio missing from the monolithic app.

### ABSTAIN Override (reasoning.py)

`calculate_evidence_score()` returns 0.00 when Gemini answers directly without calling tools. Score < 0.15 = ABSTAIN → blocks all valid business answers.

**Three-layer fix** (both streaming + non-streaming paths in `reasoning.py`):

1. `intent_classifier.py`: 17 pricing keywords in `GENERAL_TASK_KEYWORDS` trigger `skip_rag=True`
2. If `final_answer` contains pricing markers (Rp, IDR, USD), set `trusted_tools_used = True`
3. **KEY**: If LLM had `_gemini_tools` configured and produced `final_answer`, trust it → `trusted_tools_used = True`

### Rogue AI Import Removal

AI refactors (Gemini/Windsurf) silently remove `Any` from `typing` imports. `dependencies.py` is imported by EVERY router — missing `Any` crashes entire app at startup.

**Prevention**: Always run before deploy:

```bash
python -c "from backend.app.dependencies import get_current_user; print('OK')"
PYTHONPATH=. pytest backend/tests/services/rag/ -q
```

### Service Injection Pattern (avoid circular imports)

"""
Query Expander Service - Multi-Query LLM Expansion

Generates 2-3 alternative search queries using Gemini Flash to improve
recall on complex/comparative queries. Uses RRF fusion downstream.

Two expansion modes:
1. expand() — single expanded query (original + translations), backward compatible
2. expand_multi() — list of 2-3 alternative queries for RRF fusion (NEW)

Architecture:
- Gemini Flash (fast, cheap ~$0.0001/call) with 3s timeout
- Redis cache (1h TTL) to avoid repeat LLM calls
- Graceful fallback to KeywordTranslator if Gemini unavailable
- Feature flag: ENABLE_QUERY_EXPANSION

Author: RIRI (original), refactored 2026-03-24
"""

import asyncio
import json
import logging
from typing import Any

from backend.core.cache import cached

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# Prompt for multi-query expansion
_MULTI_QUERY_PROMPT = """You are a search query optimizer for an Indonesian business services platform (visa, company setup, tax, KBLI codes).

Given this user query, generate 2-3 alternative search queries that:
1. Cover different aspects or phrasings of the same intent
2. Include Indonesian legal/business terms (KITAS, PT PMA, NIB, NPWP, etc.)
3. Translate key concepts to Bahasa Indonesia (the document language)
4. Are concise and search-optimized (no full sentences)

User query: "{query}"

Return ONLY a JSON array of 2-3 strings. No explanation, no markdown:
["alternative query 1 in English/Indonesian mix", "alternative query 2", "alternative query 3"]"""

# Prompt for single translation expansion (backward compat)
_TRANSLATION_PROMPT = """Analyze this query and provide translations for search optimization.

Query: "{query}"

Respond in this EXACT JSON format only (no markdown, no code blocks):
{{"detected_lang": "xx", "english": "translation here", "indonesian": "translation here"}}

Rules:
- If query is already in English, copy it to "english" field
- If query is already in Indonesian, copy it to "indonesian" field
- Keep translations concise and search-optimized
- Preserve key terms and codes (like E25B, KITAS, etc.)"""


class QueryExpander:
    """
    Multi-query LLM expansion service.

    Expands user queries into multiple alternative search queries
    using Gemini Flash, with graceful fallback to KeywordTranslator.
    """

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._gemini: Any = None

    async def _get_gemini(self) -> Any:
        """Lazy init Gemini client."""
        if self._gemini is None:
            from backend.services.llm_clients.gemini_service import GeminiService

            self._gemini = GeminiService()
        return self._gemini

    async def _call_gemini(self, prompt: str, timeout_s: float = 3.0) -> str | None:
        """Call Gemini with timeout. Returns None on any failure."""
        try:
            client = await self._get_gemini()
            result = await asyncio.wait_for(
                client.generate_response(prompt=prompt),
                timeout=timeout_s,
            )
            return result if result and result.strip() else None
        except asyncio.TimeoutError:
            logger.warning("Gemini query expansion timed out (%.1fs)", timeout_s)
            return None
        except Exception as e:
            logger.warning("Gemini query expansion failed: %s", e)
            return None

    @staticmethod
    def _clean_json(response: str) -> str:
        """Extract JSON from potentially markdown-wrapped response."""
        clean = response.strip()
        if clean.startswith("```"):
            lines = clean.split("\n")[1:]
            if lines and lines[-1].strip() == "```":
                lines = lines[:-1]
            clean = "\n".join(lines)
        # Find JSON array or object
        for start_char, end_char in [("[", "]"), ("{", "}")]:
            start = clean.find(start_char)
            end = clean.rfind(end_char)
            if start != -1 and end > start:
                return clean[start : end + 1]
        return clean.strip()

    @cached(ttl=3600, prefix="qexp_multi")
    async def expand_multi(self, query: str) -> list[str]:
        """
        Generate 2-3 alternative search queries using Gemini Flash.

        Returns a list of queries including the original.
        Falls back to keyword-translated original on any failure.

        Args:
            query: Original user query (any language)

        Returns:
            List of 2-4 query strings (always includes original first)
        """
        # Always start with keyword-translated original
        from backend.services.search.keyword_translator import get_keyword_translator

        translator = get_keyword_translator()
        base_query = translator.translate(query)
        result_queries = [base_query]

        # Skip LLM expansion for short/simple queries
        word_count = len(query.split())
        if word_count <= 4:
            logger.debug("Query too short for LLM expansion (%d words): %s", word_count, query[:50])
            return result_queries

        # Call Gemini for multi-query expansion
        prompt = _MULTI_QUERY_PROMPT.format(query=query)
        response = await self._call_gemini(prompt, timeout_s=3.0)

        if response is None:
            logger.debug("Gemini unavailable, using keyword-only expansion")
            return result_queries

        # Parse JSON array response
        try:
            cleaned = self._clean_json(response)
            alternatives = json.loads(cleaned)
            if isinstance(alternatives, list):
                for alt in alternatives[:3]:

   171	        """Close cached Qdrant clients and clear the lazy collection cache."""
   172	        for name, client in list(self._collections_cache.items()):
   173	            close_fn = getattr(client, "close", None)
   174	            if close_fn is None:
   175	                continue
   176	            try:
   177	                close_result = close_fn()
   178	                if isawaitable(close_result):
   179	                    await close_result
   180	                logger.debug("✅ Closed collection client: %s", name)
   181	            except Exception as e:
   182	                logger.warning("⚠️ Error closing collection client '%s': %s", name, e)

  1258	    async def hybrid_search(
  1259	        self,
  1260	        query_embedding: list[float],
  1261	        query_sparse: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
  1262	        filter: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
  1263	        limit: int = 5,
  1264	        prefetch_limit: int = 20,
  1265	    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
  1266	        """
  1267	        Hybrid search combining dense and sparse (BM25) vectors with RRF fusion.
  1268	
  1269	        Uses Qdrant's prefetch + fusion API for optimal hybrid search.
  1270	
  1271	        Args:
  1272	            query_embedding: Dense query embedding vector (1536 dims)
  1273	            query_sparse: Sparse query vector {"indices": [...], "values": [...]}
  1274	            filter: Metadata filter (simplified format)
  1275	            limit: Maximum number of final results
  1276	            prefetch_limit: Number of candidates to prefetch from each vector type
  1277	
  1278	        Returns:
  1279	            Dictionary with search results in standard format
  1280	        """
  1281	        # If no sparse vector provided, fall back to dense-only search
  1282	        if not query_sparse or not query_sparse.get("indices"):
  1283	            return await self.search(query_embedding, filter=filter, limit=limit)
  1284	
  1285	        async def _do_hybrid_search():
  1286	            client = await self._get_client()
  1287	            url = f"/collections/{self.collection_name}/points/query"
  1288	
  1289	            # Build prefetch queries for both dense and sparse
  1290	            prefetch = [
  1291	                {
  1292	                    "query": query_embedding,
  1293	                    "using": "dense",
  1294	                    "limit": prefetch_limit,
  1295	                },
  1296	                {
  1297	                    "query": {
  1298	                        "indices": query_sparse["indices"],
  1299	                        "values": query_sparse["values"],
  1300	                    },
  1301	                    "using": "bm25",
  1302	                    "limit": prefetch_limit,
  1303	                },
  1304	            ]
  1305	
  1306	            # Main query with RRF fusion
  1307	            payload = {
  1308	                "prefetch": prefetch,
  1309	                "query": {"fusion": "rrf"},  # Reciprocal Rank Fusion
  1310	                "limit": limit,
  1311	                "with_payload": True,
  1312	            }
  1313	
  1314	            # Add filter if provided
  1315	            if filter:
  1316	                qdrant_filter = self._convert_filter_to_qdrant_format(
  1317	                    filter,
  1318	                    include_flat_payload=self._include_flat_payload_filters(),
  1319	                )
  1320	                if qdrant_filter:
  1321	                    payload["filter"] = qdrant_filter
  1322	
  1323	            try:
  1324	                response = await client.post(url, json=payload)
  1325	                response.raise_for_status()
  1326	
  1327	                data = response.json()
  1328	                results = data.get("result", {}).get("points", [])
  1329	
  1330	                # Transform results to standard format
  1331	                formatted_results = {
  1332	                    "ids": [str(r["id"]) for r in results],
  1333	                    "documents": [r["payload"].get("text", "") for r in results],
  1334	                    "metadatas": [_extract_point_metadata(r["payload"]) for r in results],
  1335	                    "distances": [1.0 - r.get("score", 0) for r in results],
  1336	                    "scores": [r.get("score", 0) for r in results],
  1337	                    "total_found": len(results),
  1338	                    "search_type": "hybrid_rrf",
  1339	                }
  1340	
  1341	                logger.debug(
  1342	                    f"Hybrid search: collection={self.collection_name}, "
  1343	                    f"found {len(results)} results (RRF fusion)",
  1344	                )
  1345	                return formatted_results
  1346	
  1347	            except httpx.HTTPStatusError as e:
  1348	                error_text = e.response.text if hasattr(e.response, "text") else str(e.response)
  1349	                error_lower = error_text.lower()
  1350	                # If hybrid search fails (e.g., collection doesn't have sparse/named vectors),
  1351	                # fall back to dense-only search
  1352	                # Covers: "sparse" errors, "dense" errors, "Vector params" errors
  1353	                if e.response.status_code == 400 and (
  1354	                    "sparse" in error_lower
  1355	                    or "dense" in error_lower
  1356	                    or "vector params" in error_lower
  1357	                ):
  1358	                    logger.warning(
  1359	                        f"Hybrid search not available for {self.collection_name}, "
  1360	                        "falling back to dense search",
  1361	                    )
  1362	                    return await self.search(query_embedding, filter=filter, limit=limit)
  1363	                logger.error(
  1364	                    f"Qdrant hybrid search failed: {e.response.status_code} - {error_text}",
  1365	                )
  1366	                return {
  1367	                    "ids": [],
  1368	                    "documents": [],
  1369	                    "metadatas": [],
  1370	                    "distances": [],
  1371	                    "scores": [],
  1372	                    "total_found": 0,
  1373	                    "search_type": "hybrid_rrf",
  1374	                    "error": error_text,
  1375	                }
  1376	            except Exception as e:
  1377	                logger.error("Qdrant hybrid search request error: %s", e)
  1378	                raise QdrantConnectionError(f"Qdrant connection error: {e}") from e
  1379	
  1380	        start_time = time.time()
  1381	        # 🔍 TRACING: Span for Qdrant hybrid search
  1382	        with trace_span(
  1383	            "qdrant.hybrid_search",
  1384	            {
  1385	                "collection": self.collection_name,
  1386	                "limit": limit,
  1387	                "prefetch_limit": prefetch_limit,
  1388	                "has_filter": bool(filter),
  1389	                "search_type": "hybrid_rrf",
  1390	            },
  1391	        ):
  1392	            try:
  1393	                result = await _retry_with_backoff(_do_hybrid_search)
  1394	                elapsed = time.time() - start_time
  1395	                _qdrant_metrics["search_calls"] += 1
  1396	                _qdrant_metrics["search_total_time"] += elapsed
  1397	                set_span_attribute("results_count", result.get("total_found", 0))
  1398	                set_span_attribute("latency_ms", int(elapsed * 1000))
  1399	                set_span_status("ok")
  1400	                logger.debug(f"Qdrant hybrid search completed in {elapsed * 1000:.2f}ms")
  1401	                return result
  1402	            except Exception as e:
  1403	                elapsed = time.time() - start_time
  1404	                _qdrant_metrics["errors"] += 1
  1405	                set_span_status("error", str(e))
  1406	                logger.error("Qdrant hybrid search error after retries: %s", e, exc_info=True)
  1407	                # Fall back to dense search on error
  1408	                return await self.search(query_embedding, filter=filter, limit=limit)

   166	    @cached(ttl=3600, prefix="query_expand")
   167	    async def expand(self, query: str) -> str:
   168	        """
   169	        Expand query to multiple languages (backward compatible).
   170	
   171	        Returns a single expanded string: "{original} {english} {indonesian}".
   172	        Falls back to keyword-translated query on any error.
   173	
   174	        Args:
   175	            query: Original user query in any language
   176	
   177	        Returns:
   178	            Expanded query string
   179	        """
   180	        # Keyword translation first (always works)
   181	        from backend.services.search.keyword_translator import get_keyword_translator
   182	
   183	        translator = get_keyword_translator()
   184	        base = translator.translate(query)
   185	
   186	        # Skip LLM for very short queries
   187	        if not query or len(query.strip()) < 3:
   188	            return base
   189	
   190	        # Try Gemini for translation
   191	        prompt = _TRANSLATION_PROMPT.format(query=query)
   192	        response = await self._call_gemini(prompt, timeout_s=3.0)
   193	
   194	        if response is None:
   195	            return base
   196	
   197	        try:
   198	            cleaned = self._clean_json(response)
   199	            result = json.loads(cleaned)
   200	            english = result.get("english", "").strip()
   201	            indonesian = result.get("indonesian", "").strip()
   202	
   203	            parts = [base]
   204	            if english and english.lower() != query.lower():
   205	                parts.append(english)
   206	            if indonesian and indonesian.lower() not in (query.lower(), english.lower()):
   207	                parts.append(indonesian)
   208	
   209	            expanded = " ".join(parts)
   210	            logger.info("Query translated: '%s' → %d chars", query[:30], len(expanded))
   211	            return expanded
   212	        except json.JSONDecodeError:
   213	            return base
