   427	
   428	    # Extract meaningful keywords: not a stop word, and either long enough to
   429	    # be unlikely to collide OR a known short identifier.
   430	    #
   431	    # WHY (spec criterion 2, measured live 2026-09-01): the `len(w) > 3` rule
   432	    # this replaces discarded every token of three characters or fewer, which
   433	    # in THIS business throws away the subject of the sentence.
   434	    # "Harga PT PMA berapa all in?" reduced to `harga` and `berapa` — the two
   435	    # most generic words in it — while `PT`, `PMA`, `NIB` and `OSS` vanished.
   436	    # `KITAS`, `NPWP` and `E28A` survived only by an accident of length.
   437	    #
   438	    # It also matters far beyond token count: a business identifier is spelled
   439	    # the SAME in every language, so it is the natural bridge between an
   440	    # Indonesian question and an English chunk. Keeping it is what lets the
   441	    # lexical ratio see across languages at all — measured, it closes seven of
   442	    # the eight cross-language cases in the golden set.
   443	    #
   444	    # A rule keyed on token LENGTH cannot express "keep the entity", so it is
   445	    # now keyed on VOCABULARY.
   446	    query_words = [
   447	        stripped
   448	        for w in query_lower.split()
   449	        if (stripped := w.strip(".,!?;:()[]{}\"'")) not in stop_words
   450	        and (len(stripped) > 3 or stripped in _SHORT_IDENTIFIERS)
   451	    ]
   452	
   453	    # Remove duplicates while preserving order
   454	    seen = set()
   455	    query_keywords = []
   456	    for w in query_words:
   457	        # The vocabulary exemption has to be repeated here: this second filter
   458	        # is `len(w) > 2`, so without it every TWO-character identifier the
   459	        # extractor above just kept — `PT` above all, the one the spec names
   460	        # first — was silently dropped again one loop later. Measured
   461	        # 2026-09-03 on this branch before the fix: `pt`, `rp`, `sk`, `cv`,
   462	        # `b1`, `c1`, `c2`, `c7`, `d1`, `d2` never reached the matcher at all.
   463	        if w not in seen and (len(w) > 2 or w in _SHORT_IDENTIFIERS):
   464	            seen.add(w)
   465	            query_keywords.append(w)
   466	
   467	    # Combine all context for analysis
   468	    context_text = " ".join(context_gathered).lower() if context_gathered else ""
   469	
   470	    # ========== RELEVANCE SCORING ==========
   471	    # Calculate keyword match ratio - how many query keywords appear in context
   472	    # EVERY identifier in the vocabulary is matched on WORD BOUNDARIES; only
   473	    # ordinary keywords keep substring matching.
   474	    #
   475	    # The split is by VOCABULARY, not by length, and that distinction is the
   476	    # whole finding. A first version of this guard drew the line at two
   477	    # characters, on the reasoning that only the shortest tokens collide. It
   478	    # is false, and measured: `oss` is inside `loss`, `cross`, `across`,
   479	    # `possible`; `hak` is inside `shake`; `imb` is inside `climb` and `limb`;
   480	    # `pt` is inside `receipt`, `script`, `empty`, `accept`; `rp` is inside
   481	    # `corporate`. Measured on this tree before the fix, with no sources at
   482	    # all: "Apa itu OSS?" against a chunk about quarterly earnings scored
   483	    # **0.600**, and "Apa itu hak milik?" against a chunk about shaking a
   484	    # bottle scored **0.600** — both far over the 0.15 ABSTAIN gate, on text
   485	    # with no topical relation whatever.
   486	    #
   487	    # Substring matching stays for ordinary keywords because Indonesian
   488	    # morphology depends on it (`pendirian` inside `pendiriannya`). An
   489	    # identifier does not inflect, so it loses nothing by being matched whole.
   490	    #
   491	    # PERF: the whole-context tokenisation only matters for the (rare) query
   492	    # that actually names a vocabulary identifier — most queries contain
   493	    # none, and `re.findall` over the full joined context is the one
   494	    # non-trivial cost in this function. Computed lazily, on the first
   495	    # identifier lookup, and cached so the two `_hits` call sites (query
   496	    # keywords, then the legacy keyword set below) share one pass. Behaviour
   497	    # is identical to the eager version; only the WHEN changes.
   498	    _context_tokens_cache: set[str] | None = None
   499	
   500	    def _context_tokens() -> set[str]:
   501	        nonlocal _context_tokens_cache
   502	        if _context_tokens_cache is None:
   503	            _context_tokens_cache = set(re.findall(r"[a-z0-9]+", context_text))
   504	        return _context_tokens_cache
   505	
   506	    def _hits(kw: str) -> bool:
   507	        return kw in _context_tokens() if kw in _SHORT_IDENTIFIERS else kw in context_text
   508	
   509	    keyword_hits = sum(1 for kw in query_keywords if _hits(kw))
   510	
   511	    keyword_match_ratio = keyword_hits / len(query_keywords) if query_keywords else 0.0
   512	
   513	    # ---- THE VOCABULARY MAY ONLY ADD EVIDENCE, NEVER SUBTRACT IT ----------
   514	    #
   515	    # The ratio is hits/keywords, so every token this change newly KEEPS also
   516	    # enlarges the denominator. On a chunk that answers part of a composite
   517	    # question, that reads as less evidence than before — the opposite of the
   518	    # intent. Measured on the same relevant KITAS chunk, before this clamp:
   519	    # "syarat visa KITAS" scored 0.600, "syarat visa KITAS NIB OSS" 0.400, and
   520	    # a twelve-identifier question **0.000 — below the gate, where the old
   521	    # rule scored 0.600**. Naming more of the right things made the bot
   522	    # abstain.
   523	    #
   524	    # Dilution is inherited (it is what hits/keywords means, and it applied to
   525	    # ordinary keywords long before this change); what this change controls is
   526	    # how many tokens enter the denominator. So the ratio is computed a second
   527	    # time over exactly the token set the OLD length rule would have produced,
   528	    # and the better of the two wins. The floor is therefore, by construction
   529	    # and not by testing, the score this function returned before the
   530	    # vocabulary existed: adding an identifier can raise a score and can never
   531	    # lower one.
   532	    #
   533	    # ONE DECLARED EXCEPTION, and it is deliberate: the legacy ratio reuses
   534	    # `_hits`, so it inherits the old TOKEN SET but not the old MATCHING. The
   535	    # old length rule was applied to the RAW token, so a three-letter
   536	    # identifier written with punctuation behind it — `OSS?` is four
   537	    # characters — passed it, and was then matched as a substring. Measured:
   538	    # "Apa itu OSS?" against a chunk about a quarterly net LOSS scores 0.600
   539	    # on origin/main and 0.000 here. That is the only direction in which this
   540	    # function may now return less than it used to, it is a precision gain on
   541	    # a safety gate, and it is pinned by a test rather than left to be
   542	    # rediscovered as a regression.
   543	    legacy_seen: set[str] = set()
   544	    legacy_keywords: list[str] = []
   545	    for w in query_lower.split():
   546	        if len(w) > 3 and (stripped := w.strip(".,!?;:()[]{}\"'")) not in stop_words:
   547	            if stripped not in legacy_seen and len(stripped) > 2:
   548	                legacy_seen.add(stripped)
   549	                legacy_keywords.append(stripped)
   550	    if legacy_keywords:
   551	        legacy_hits = sum(1 for kw in legacy_keywords if _hits(kw))
   552	        keyword_match_ratio = max(keyword_match_ratio, legacy_hits / len(legacy_keywords))
   553	
   554	    # Check for specific entity type mismatches (e.g., KITAS query returning KBLI)
   555	    # This catches cases where the topic is completely wrong
   556	    entity_type_mismatch = False
   557	    if query_keywords:
   558	        # Define topic categories
   559	        visa_keywords = {"kitas", "kitap", "visa", "immigration", "imigrasi", "permit", "stay"}
   560	        kbli_keywords = {"kbli", "classification", "business", "code", "kode", "klasifikasi"}
   561	        tax_keywords = {"tax", "tasse", "pajak", "fiscal", "fiscale", "npwp", "pph"}
   562	        company_keywords = {"pt", "pma", "company", "azienda", "usaha", "perusahaan"}
   563	
   564	        query_has_visa = any(kw in visa_keywords for kw in query_keywords)
   565	        query_has_kbli = any(kw in kbli_keywords for kw in query_keywords)
   566	        query_has_tax = any(kw in tax_keywords for kw in query_keywords)
   567	        query_has_company = any(kw in company_keywords for kw in query_keywords)
   568	
   569	        context_has_visa = any(kw in context_text for kw in visa_keywords)
   570	        context_has_kbli = any(kw in context_text for kw in kbli_keywords)
   571	        context_has_tax = any(kw in context_text for kw in tax_keywords)
   572	        context_has_company = any(kw in context_text for kw in company_keywords)
   573	
   574	        # Detect mismatch: query about X but context about Y (where X != Y)
   575	        # Only flag if context clearly belongs to a different category
   576	        if query_has_visa and (context_has_kbli or context_has_tax) and not context_has_visa:
   577	            entity_type_mismatch = True
   578	        if query_has_kbli and (context_has_visa or context_has_tax) and not context_has_kbli:
   579	            entity_type_mismatch = True
   580	        if query_has_tax and (context_has_visa or context_has_kbli) and not context_has_tax:
   581	            entity_type_mismatch = True
   582	        if query_has_company and (context_has_visa or context_has_tax) and not context_has_company:
   583	            entity_type_mismatch = True
   584	
   585	        # If mismatch detected, force very low semantic relevance
   586	        if entity_type_mismatch:
   587	            keyword_match_ratio = min(keyword_match_ratio, 0.1)
   588	
   589	    # Semantic relevance score (0.0 - 0.6)
   590	    # Relevance is the PRIMARY factor - source quality only helps if relevance is good
   591	    # - Full match: keyword_match_ratio >= 0.5 → 0.6 points
   592	    # - Good match: keyword_match_ratio >= 0.3 → 0.4 points
   593	    # - Partial match: keyword_match_ratio >= 0.15 → 0.2 points
   594	    # - Poor match: keyword_match_ratio < 0.15 → 0.0 points (ABSTAIN territory)
   595	    if keyword_match_ratio >= 0.5:
   596	        semantic_relevance = 0.6  # Strong relevance
   597	    elif keyword_match_ratio >= 0.3:
   598	        semantic_relevance = 0.4  # Moderate relevance
   599	    elif keyword_match_ratio >= 0.15:
   600	        semantic_relevance = 0.2  # Weak relevance
   601	    else:
   602	        semantic_relevance = 0.0  # No relevant keywords found
   603	
   604	    # ========== SOURCE QUALITY SCORING ==========
   605	    # Source quality is SECONDARY - it can only boost score if relevance exists
   606	    source_quality_score = 0.0
   607	
   608	    if sources:
   609	        # Get top source score (best evidence)
   610	        source_scores = [s.get("score", 0.0) for s in sources if isinstance(s, dict)]
   550	    retrieved.sort(key=lambda c: (-float(c["score"]), c["collection"], c["text"]))
   551	    chunks.extend(retrieved)
   552	
   553	    chunks = _cap_chunks(chunks)
   554	
   555	    pricing_block: dict[str, Any] | None = None
   556	    if has_pricing_intent(query):
   557	        pricing_block = _sanitize_pricing_block(get_pricing_service().search_service(query))
   558	
   559	    payload_history = _sanitize_history(history, query)
   560	
   561	    # G-P3 DLP gate — runs AFTER chunks are capped and history sanitized,
   562	    # BEFORE evidence_inputs/package_hash are computed: everything hashed
   563	    # and sealed below is the REDACTED content, never the original (spec
   564	    # rule: "package_hash computed over the REDACTED content"). Fail-closed
   565	    # by construction — ANY exception from the redact step (detector bug,
   566	    # or wa_dlp's own >MAX_PLACEHOLDERS overflow guard) becomes
   567	    # PackageUnbuildable so the row falls off to the Gemini leg instead of
   568	    # shipping a partially-redacted or unredacted package.
   569	    #
   570	    # M1 (Kimi adversarial review, verified): `pricing_block["search_query"]`
   571	    # is the customer's RAW query, verbatim (pricing_service.py sets it from
   572	    # the same `query` argument `_sanitize_pricing_block` keeps untouched) —
   573	    # without threading it through the SAME redaction state as history, a
   574	    # NIK in the query would be redacted in `history[-1]["content"]` but
   575	    # ship unredacted a few keys over in `pricing_block.search_query`, into
   576	    # both the wire and the hash. Passed into `redact_package_fields` so it
   577	    # shares dedup/placeholder-numbering with the history copy of the same
   578	    # string, then spliced back before hashing.
   579	    reversal_map: dict[str, str] = {}
   580	    if dlp:
   581	        pricing_search_query = (
   582	            pricing_block.get("search_query") if pricing_block is not None else None
   583	        )
   584	        try:
   585	            dlp_result = redact_package_fields(payload_history, chunks, pricing_search_query)
   586	        except Exception as exc:
   587	            logger.info(
   588	                "wa_package_builder: dlp redaction failed error=%s",
   589	                type(exc).__name__,
   590	            )
   591	            raise PackageUnbuildable(reason="dlp_error") from exc
   592	        payload_history = dlp_result.history
   593	        chunks = dlp_result.chunks
   594	        reversal_map = dlp_result.reversal_map
   595	        if pricing_block is not None:
   596	            pricing_block = {**pricing_block, "search_query": dlp_result.search_query}
   597	
   598	    persona_digest = _build_persona_digest()
   599	
   600	    # Evidence inputs are FROZEN here — finalization (spec §2.3) reads these
   601	    # fields, it never recomputes them against a possibly-drifted retrieval.
   602	    abstain_policy = build_abstain_policy(query)
   603	    evidence_score = calculate_evidence_score(
   604	        sources=[
   605	            {
   606	                "score": chunk["score"],
   607	                "score_kind": score_provenance.kind_of(chunk),
   608	                "score_raw": score_provenance.raw_of(chunk),
   609	            }
   610	            for chunk in chunks
   611	        ],
   612	        context_gathered=[chunk["text"] for chunk in chunks],
   613	        query=query,
   614	    )
   615	    evidence_inputs: dict[str, Any] = {
   616	        "evidence_score": evidence_score,
   617	        "context_length": len(chunks),
   618	        "domain": plan.domain.value,
   619	        "label_threshold": abstain_policy.label_threshold,
   620	        "abstain": abstain_policy.label_abstains(evidence_score),
   621	        # Kimi-7 (MINOR): inside the hash domain by design — a future
   622	        # caller that forgets `dlp=True` produces a package distinguishable
   623	        # downstream (evidence_inputs is what finalize/consumers read out
   624	        # of the sealed wire) instead of one indistinguishable from a
   625	        # properly-redacted build.
   626	        "dlp": dlp,
   627	    }
   628	
   629	    package_hash = _package_hash(
   630	        history=payload_history,
   631	        chunks=chunks,
   632	        pricing_block=pricing_block,
   633	        persona_digest=persona_digest,
   634	        evidence_inputs=evidence_inputs,
   635	        thread_epoch=thread_epoch,
   636	    )

+# B1.2 inventory — the eleven, by inventory and not by grep
+
+Base sha `1d726045c9`. Every line below was re-read from disk on this base by the Dux, not
+taken from a child's report. Paths are relative to `apps/backend-rag/backend/tests/`.
+
+## The nine "impossible cosine" fixtures — RECOVERED
+
+| #   | node id                                                                                                                                                 | line     | literal today                                                   |
+| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
+| 1   | `unit/services/rag/agentic/test_abstain_bypass_policy.py::TestTrustedToolDetection::test_successful_but_irrelevant_vector_hit_stays_below_abstain_gate` | :294     | `sources=[{"score": 0.91}]`                                     |
+| 2   | `unit/services/rag/agentic/test_reasoning.py::TestCalculateEvidenceScore::test_no_keyword_overlap`                                                      | :212     | `sources = [{"score": 0.9}]`                                    |
+| 3   | `services/rag/agentic/test_reasoning_utils.py::TestCalculateEvidenceScore::test_stop_words_only_query_keyword_ratio_zero`                               | :196     | `[{"score": 0.8}]`                                              |
+| 4   | `services/rag/agentic/test_reasoning_utils.py::TestCalculateEvidenceScore::test_short_words_only_yields_near_zero`                                      | :207     | `[{"score": 0.8}]`                                              |
+| 5   | `services/rag/agentic/test_reasoning_utils.py::TestCalculateEvidenceScore::test_entity_mismatch_company_vs_visa`                                        | :256     | `[{"score": 0.6}]`                                              |
+| 6   | `services/rag/agentic/test_reasoning_utils.py::TestCalculateEvidenceScore::test_semantic_penalty_not_applied_when_final_score_at_or_below_015`          | :284     | `[{"score": 0.35}]  # cosine 0.35 < 0.5, would trigger penalty` |
+| 7   | `services/rag/test_evidence_scoring_abstain.py::TestEvidenceScoringFixed::test_kitas_query_with_kbli_results_low_score`                                 | :34, :35 | `"score": 0.85` and `"score": 0.75` (two sources, one test)     |
+| 8   | `services/rag/test_evidence_scoring_abstain.py::TestEvidenceScoringFixed::test_nonsense_query_zero_score`                                               | :57      | `"score": 0.8`                                                  |
+| 9   | `services/rag/test_evidence_scoring_abstain.py::TestEvidenceScoringFixed::test_entity_type_mismatch_detection`                                          | :148     | `sources = [{"id": 1, "score": 0.9}]`                           |
+
+**Excluded on purpose, verified:** `test_abstain_bypass_policy.py:311` also reads `0.91`, but it
+belongs to `test_relevant_vector_hit_can_pass_through_relevance_path`, a different and untouched
+test that expects the score to PASS. A score-literal grep would have swept it in; that is the
+concrete reason D3 forbids the grep as an inventory method.
+
+**Row 6 is the corroboration of F-B1.1a, in the repo's own words.** Its inline comment says
+`cosine 0.35 < 0.5, would trigger penalty` — the fixture exists to exercise
+`reasoning_utils.py:665`, and the measured pipeline floor of 0.5082 says that branch cannot be
+entered from any live path. The fixture is not merely using an unreachable number; it is the only
+witness to a guard production never runs.
+
+## The tenth — NAMED, NOT LOCATED
+
+PR #5618's body, verbatim: _"It regressed eleven standing abstain tripwires: nine let an off-topic
+query clear the gate on cosine alone, and one newly discarded a perfect lexical match (over-abstain,
+the opposite direction)."_ That tenth is a behaviour gap in the withdrawn design, not a fictional
+cosine, so it is **not** a fixture re-base candidate. No file or line for it appears in any source.
+
+## The eleventh — UNRESOLVED
+
+Three independent sources — the #5618 PR body, `evidence/2026-09/agent-nuzantara-ops-bot-wa-evidence-e34da839/brief.yml:26-31`,
+and the rc1a commit message — all assert **eleven** and all itemise **nine plus one**. 9 + 1 = 10.
+Nothing anywhere names the eleventh. Recorded as explicitly unresolved per D3, never guessed.
+
+## The withdrawn candidate SHA — UNRESOLVED, with an immutable receipt
+
+Not in #5618's eight pre-squash commits, not in `git log --all`, not in `git fsck --unreachable`,
+not findable by GitHub commit search on its distinctive phrases. Receipt in lieu of the SHA:
+`gh api repos/Bali-Zero/Teman2/actions/runs?branch=agent/nuzantara/ops/bot-wa-evidence&per_page=100`
+over three pages returns **255 runs spanning 2026-09-03T11:35Z → 2026-09-09T17:17Z whose
+conclusions are only `success` or `cancelled` — zero `failure`, ever.** #5618 was squash-merged as
+`2cd3cf84b8` and its branch ref deleted. So the verifier run that found the eleven regressions was
+never a CI run: it ran out of band and its pytest output was never committed. That is the honest
+end of the trail, and it is why the eleventh cannot be recovered rather than merely has not been.
+
+## The sibling artifact B1.2 consumes — and the reason it cannot be adopted as it stands
+
+The branch carries **three** unpushed commits, and the pack cites all three (the first draft of
+this inventory recorded only the first — the staff room caught it, and the correction matters
+because the second commit is a design question, not a duplicate):
+
+| sha                                        | subject                                                                          | what it is                                         |
+| ------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
+| `92f40801235cf33b32a8d71f241b6400cad64536` | test(bot): rebase nine abstain tripwires on measured embedding cosines           | the rebase + `measured_cosines.py`                 |
+| `30a0205dab`                               | test(bot): guard abstain fixtures against unmeasured cosines                     | a 192-line guard, `test_measured_cosines_guard.py` |
+| `84bf272a24`                               | docs(evidence): brief for the nine abstain tripwires rebased on measured cosines | the lane's brief; branch tip                       |
+
+A local keep-ref `refs/keep/rc1a-2026-09-11 -> 84bf272a24` protects all three from a broker reap or
+a graveyard sweep. **Read the table by sha** (`git show 92f4080123:apps/backend-rag/backend/tests/fixtures/measured_cosines.py`),
+never from the working tree.
+
+### The guard commit inherits the same false premise, and B1's own vocabulary is what refutes it
+
+`30a0205dab`'s message says it "flagged exactly the seven >= 0.8 fixtures of the nine and nothing
+else in backend/tests" — that is a guard that judges a score by its MAGNITUDE, on the assumption
+that a high number is an implausible cosine. Under the measured pipeline a high number is entirely
+plausible: a result ranked first in BOTH prefetch lists fuses to 1.0 and formats to 1.0, and the
+curated block is minted at a literal 1.0. So that guard would flag legitimate pipeline values while
+passing a fictional 0.4. `core/score_provenance.py`'s first rule already states the principle that
+refutes it — a kind is declared by the writer that mints the value and is NEVER inferred from
+magnitude, because magnitudes overlap across kinds by construction.
+
+**B1.2 keeps the guard's INTENT and inverts its test.** The guard B1.2 ships is the one `B1-design.md`
+§4 specifies: it fails if a tripwire fixture declares a source `score` without a `score_kind` from
+the frozen vocabulary. Provenance-based, not magnitude-based — it cannot be fooled by a plausible
+number and cannot punish an implausible-looking true one.
+
+### The rebase commit
+
+Commit `92f40801235cf33b32a8d71f241b6400cad64536` (worktree
+`.worktrees/backend-rag-rc1a-fixtures-real-cosines`, branch
+`agent/nuzantara/backend-rag/rc1a-fixtures-real-cosines`, **never pushed**, broker lease ORPHAN)
+rebases these same nine onto real `text-embedding-3-small` cosines measured in one API request
+(20 texts, 216 prompt tokens) inside the prod `rag` container at 2026-09-10T18:03Z.
+
+Its own docstring states the premise the whole commit rests on: _"A `sources[].score` in a
+scorer fixture stands for the cosine the retriever would return for (query, retrieved chunk)."_
+**That premise is false.** `sources[].score` is what `wa_package_builder.py:289` forwards out of
+`result_formatter.py:89` — `1/(1+distance)` over a server-side RRF output on the hybrid path,
+`1/(2-cos)` on the dense path. It is never a cosine on either. Its measured values span 0.04-0.51
+and the measured live floor is 0.5082 hybrid / 0.5 dense, so **every one of them is below the
+floor**: as impossible as the 0.35-0.91 they replace, in the other direction.
+
+Two further divergences from D3/D5: the commit MOVES the inputs in place ("only the fictional
+inputs moved") where D3 requires the originals PRESERVED with variants ADDED, and its table is
+keyed on `(query, context)` text pairs so it cannot express contributing ranks, list membership or
+fusion configuration — the fields D3 makes mandatory.
+
+**What B1.2 does with it.** The cosines are sound as `score_raw` on the DENSE path, which is the
+one path with a real cosine behind the number, and the correct fixture value is one transform away:
+
+| measured cosine | → `score` = 1/(2−cos) |
+| --------------- | --------------------- |
+| 0.04            | 0.5102                |
+| 0.14            | 0.5376                |
+| 0.16            | 0.5435                |
+| 0.30            | 0.5882                |
+| 0.51            | 0.6711                |
+
+All five land above the 0.5082 hybrid floor, inside the live band. B1.2 therefore ADDS variants
+that carry the measured cosine as `score_raw`, the derived value as `score`, and
+`score_kind = dense_formatted`, beside the nine preserved originals — and makes **no new embedding
+call**, so D3's "no embedding API call is needed" holds without reopening any allowance.

+# B1.1 first artifact — the MEASURED rank ↔ returned-score table
+
+Bound to git base sha `1d726045c9ca75ab64a75e92b062e52b0b763a98`. Every citation below is a
+citation into THAT tree, never into a Fly release: prod moved from v4389 (the release live at
+measurement time) to v4396 at 20:47Z on unrelated compliance-stack deploys while this artifact
+was being written, which is exactly why the binding is to the git base.
+
+## Why this had to be measured
+
+`README.md` §0 states the fusion table as "an expectation, not a measurement": production fusion
+is server-side (`apps/backend-rag/backend/core/qdrant_db.py:1308`, `{"fusion": "rrf"}`) and the
+server's ranking constant is not verified from any file in this repo. The two in-repo RRF helpers
+(`backend/services/rag/hybrid_search.py:32`, `RRF_K = 60`, 1-based ranks; and
+`backend/services/search/search_service.py:897-916`) are NOT the hot path. The installed client's
+local-mode constant (`apps/backend-rag/.venv/lib/python3.11/site-packages/qdrant_client/hybrid/fusion.py:4-11`,
+`ranking_constant = 2`) is the package's file, not the server's behaviour.
+
+## Method
+
+Read-only, inside the prod container, machine `1781e5eda03438`.
+
+- The query vectors are **one existing point's own stored `dense` and `bm25` vectors**, fetched by
+  `points/scroll` with `with_vector=true`. **No embedding call was made.**
+- **No write** of any kind: only `GET /`, `GET /collections`, `GET /collections/{c}`,
+  `POST .../points/scroll` and `POST .../points/query`.
+- **Nothing was exported** except server version, collection name, point count, vector dimensions,
+  per-list ranks, list membership and returned scores. No payload, no point id, no vector, no
+  credential. The probe reads `QDRANT_URL`/`QDRANT_API_KEY` from the container environment and
+  never prints them.
+- The fused call is byte-shaped on the production payload at `core/qdrant_db.py:1288-1312`
+  (a `dense` + `bm25` prefetch under `{"fusion": "rrf"}`), with `prefetch_limit = limit * 3` as
+  `backend/services/search/search_service.py:1140` computes it.
+- The probe was uploaded to the container's `/tmp` with `fly ssh sftp put`, executed once with
+  `fly ssh console -C`, and deleted in a second `fly ssh console -C` call.
+
+This is the ONE prod-Qdrant read `B1-design.md` §2 grants B1 (the RC2 fence is write/export).
+Any further prod read is gated on a staff-room ack before it runs.
+
+## Measured
+
+- **Prod Qdrant server version `1.16.3`.** Pro's local Qdrant is `1.12.5`, so the local instance
+  was NOT usable as a stand-in — the version that decides the constant is the prod one.
+- Collection `kbli_2025_final_hybrid`, 1873 points, dense dim 1536, query sparse nnz 393.
+- `prefetch_limit = 30`, fused `limit = 10`.
+
+| fused_rank | dense_rank0 | bm25_rank0 | lists      | returned_score | distance | formatted |
+| ---------- | ----------- | ---------- | ---------- | -------------- | -------- | --------- |
+| 0          | 0           | 0          | dense+bm25 | 1.000000       | 0.000000 | 1.000000  |
+| 1          | 1           | 1          | dense+bm25 | 0.666667       | 0.333333 | 0.750000  |
+| 2          | 2           | 14         | dense+bm25 | 0.312500       | 0.687500 | 0.592593  |
+| 3          | 16          | 2          | dense+bm25 | 0.305556       | 0.694444 | 0.590164  |
+| 4          | 7           | 4          | dense+bm25 | 0.277778       | 0.722222 | 0.580645  |
+| 5          | 20          | 3          | dense+bm25 | 0.245455       | 0.754545 | 0.569948  |
+| 6          | 3           | —          | dense      | 0.200000       | 0.800000 | 0.555556  |
+| 7          | 4           | —          | dense      | 0.166667       | 0.833333 | 0.545455  |
+| 8          | 5           | —          | dense      | 0.142857       | 0.857143 | 0.538462  |
+| 9          | —           | 5          | bm25       | 0.142857       | 0.857143 | 0.538462  |
+
+`distance` is `1.0 - returned_score` (`core/qdrant_db.py:1335`); `formatted` is `1/(1+distance)`
+(`backend/services/misc/result_formatter.py:89`), shown WITHOUT any collection boost.
+
+## Verdict
+
+**`returned_score = Σ over contributing lists of 1 / (2 + rank0)`** — zero-based rank, ranking
+constant 2. Exact on all ten rows (`MATCHES_k2_zero_based=True`). The k=60 / 1-based hypothesis is
+refuted on all ten (`MATCHES_k60_one_based=False`).
+
+So the SERVER's constant equals the installed client's local-mode constant. The README's
+expectation table is CONFIRMED by measurement rather than assumed; there is no reconciliation gap
+to declare, and B1.1(c)'s values derive from a table that is now both measured and local. Kimi K1's
+substantive point holds: the in-repo RRF helpers are not the hot path.
+
+## The consequence — finding F-B1.1a
+
+Fusion output is bounded BELOW at `1/(2 + prefetch_limit - 1) = 1/31 = 0.0323`, so
+`distance ≤ 0.9677` and `result_formatter.py:89` maps **every** hybrid result into
+**[0.508, 1.000]** before any collection boost. The dense fallback is bounded the same way for any
+non-negative cosine: `1/(2 - cos) ≥ 0.5` at `cos = 0`. The curated block is minted at a synthetic
+`1.0` (`services/rag/agentic/wa_package_builder.py:529`).
+
+Therefore `services/rag/agentic/reasoning_utils.py:665` —
+
+```
+if 0 < top_source_cosine < 0.5 and final_score > 0.15:
+```
+
+— **cannot fire on any live path**: not hybrid, not dense-fallback-with-non-negative-cosine, not
+curated. The only input that reaches it is a NEGATIVE cosine (which makes `distance > 1`), and
+1536-dim `text-embedding-3-small` vectors essentially never produce one. The comment above it at
+`:650` calls the field "Qdrant's per-source cosine similarity"; it is a ranking transform that
+structurally cannot enter the branch written to consume it.
+
+**The semantic-alignment penalty is effectively dead code in production.** That is now a measured
+claim, not a reading, and it sharpens D1 in a way the packet should carry: repairing provenance
+does not repair the scorer, and B2 will be inverting a metric one of whose two guards has never run.
+
+It also constrains B1.4: **no support-signal candidate may lean on `top_source_cosine` as currently
+minted**, because that number is not observable as a relevance signal on the live path.
+
+## Digest-compatibility receipt (B1.1(d) input)
+
+Measured read-only on prod Postgres via `scripts/pg.sh` at the same sitting:
+
+```
+ status | count |            newest
+--------+-------+-------------------------------
+ failed |   241 | 2026-09-01 08:31:11.591666+00
+ done   |   179 | 2026-09-01 09:27:53.327524+00
+```
+
+Only terminal statuses exist; there is no `pending` or `generating` row, and the newest row of any
+kind is 2026-09-01 09:27:53Z — the same instant Fable measured independently. **No package is in
+flight**, so the "a digest change with rows in flight is a STOP" condition of `B1-design.md` §4(d)
+is not triggered. This is a receipt about in-flight rows only; it is not a licence to change the
+digest casually, and the PR states the digest's actual disposition separately.

    43	#: The key a source/chunk dict carries its pre-transform value under. Definition, and it
    44	#: is deliberately narrow: `score_raw` is the number THIS writer consumed to produce
    45	#: `score`. It is `None` when the value is synthetic (nothing was consumed) or when the
    46	#: producing step is not recoverable. It is never a second opinion about `score`.
    47	SCORE_RAW_KEY: Final = "score_raw"

    49	#: Server-side Reciprocal Rank Fusion (`core/qdrant_db.py:1308`) put through
    50	#: `result_formatter.py:89`. `score_raw` is the fusion output, in (0, 2].
    51	HYBRID_RRF_FORMATTED: Final = "hybrid_rrf_formatted"
    52	
    53	#: A dense-only cosine put through the same formatter — either the explicit dense branch
    54	#: of `search_service` or the internal fallback at `core/qdrant_db.py:1362`. `score_raw`
    55	#: is the cosine.
    56	DENSE_FORMATTED: Final = "dense_formatted"
    57	
    58	#: A reranker overwrote `score` with its own relevance score (`core/reranker.py:169-180`,
    59	#: `services/rag/reranker.py:345-352`). `score_raw` is the value that reranking pass
    60	#: replaced — on a FIRST rerank that is the retrieval score those writers also keep under
    61	#: `vector_score`, but a result reranked twice carries the previous rerank's output, so
    62	#: `score_raw` and `vector_score` are not interchangeable in general.
    63	RERANKED: Final = "reranked"

    65	#: The curated-QA block minted at a literal 1.0 by `wa_package_builder.py:529`. Nothing was

    67	CURATED_SYNTHETIC: Final = "curated_synthetic"

    75	TRUSTED_TOOL_BYPASS: Final = "trusted_tool_bypass"

    92	
    93	#: The kinds that have real retrieval behind them, so a numeric comparison between two of
    94	#: them means something. `CURATED_SYNTHETIC` and `TRUSTED_TOOL_BYPASS` are deliberately
    95	#: absent: they are labels, not measurements.
    96	RETRIEVED_KINDS: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    97	    {HYBRID_RRF_FORMATTED, DENSE_FORMATTED, RERANKED},
    98	)
    99	
   100	
   101	def kind_of(source: Any) -> str:
   102	    """Read a source's declared kind, defaulting to `UNKNOWN`.
   103	
   104	    Accepts anything, because the projections this travels through are typed `list[Any]`
   105	    and a non-dict source has, by definition, declared nothing.
   106	    """
   107	    if not isinstance(source, dict):
   108	        return UNKNOWN
   109	    kind = source.get(SCORE_KIND_KEY)
   110	    return kind if kind in SCORE_KINDS else UNKNOWN
   111	
   112	
   113	def raw_of(source: Any) -> float | None:
   114	    """Read a source's pre-transform value, or `None` when it has none or is unreadable."""
   115	    if not isinstance(source, dict):
   116	        return None
   117	    raw = source.get(SCORE_RAW_KEY)
   118	    return float(raw) if isinstance(raw, (int, float)) else None
   119	
   120	
   121	def stamp(
   122	    target: dict[str, Any],
   123	    kind: str,
   124	    raw: float | None = None,
   125	) -> dict[str, Any]:
   126	    """Declare a kind on a dict IN PLACE, additively, and return it.
   127	
   128	    Raises on an unknown kind: a writer that invents a spelling is a writer whose value the
   129	    consumers cannot read, and failing at the mint is cheaper than an `UNKNOWN` appearing
   130	    three layers downstream with nothing to trace it to.
   131	    """
   132	    if kind not in SCORE_KINDS:
   133	        raise ValueError(
   134	            f"unknown score_kind {kind!r}; the vocabulary is {sorted(SCORE_KINDS)}",
   135	        )
   136	    target[SCORE_KIND_KEY] = kind
   137	    if raw is not None:
   138	        target[SCORE_RAW_KEY] = float(raw)
   139	    return target

Revisione dei due diff su `f56d3e5dc1`, senza modifiche. I riferimenti seguenti sono relativi ad `apps/backend-rag/backend/`.

1. **NOT-A-DEFECT — dichiarazione completa in `search()`.**  
   I tre percorsi assegnano `declared_kind`: successo hybrid a `services/search/search_service.py:672`, fallback dense a `:693`, BM25 disabilitato a `:715`, prima del formatter a `:718`. La discriminante è correttamente la **presenza** di `search_type`, coerente con `:1180` e con il produttore Qdrant (`core/qdrant_db.py:1338`, fallback a `:1362`). L’espansione multipla restituisce subito a `:609`; risultato vuoto o eccezione proseguono attraverso le assegnazioni ordinarie (`:608–611`). Gli altri errori fanno uscire la funzione o propagano: nessun percorso raggiunge il formatter con variabile non assegnata. Non esiste un loop che possa conservarne il valore precedente.

2. **NOT-A-DEFECT — gli altri tre produttori sono dense-only.**  
   `_single_query_search` chiama esclusivamente `vector_db.search` (`services/search/search_service.py:891`); `search_single_collection` altrettanto (`:1458`); `search_collection` chiama `client.search` (`:1650`). Il metodo Qdrant usa `/points/search` e una query dense (`core/qdrant_db.py:489–499`). La fusione dell’espansione multipla aggiunge separatamente `rrf_score`, senza sostituire `score` (`services/search/search_service.py:941–942`): non rende errata la provenienza dichiarata del campo `score`.

3. **NOT-A-DEFECT — nessun chiamante produttivo esterno dimenticato.**  
   Il grep dell’intero backend trova cinque invocazioni produttive, tutte in `services/search/search_service.py`: `:718`, `:900`, `:1201`, `:1468`, `:1657`; tutte dichiarano il tipo. Fuori dal file restano import/export, commenti, docstring e test. Alcuni test preesistenti esercitano ancora il default, per esempio `tests/services/misc/test_result_formatter.py:15`: non costituiscono un produttore live privo di dichiarazione.

4. **NOT-A-DEFECT — oracle precedente verificato e rifiuto pertinente.**  
   Ho ricalcolato i literal usando **le funzioni del commit base `1d726045c9`**, estratte con `git show`. Risultato: **320 byte**, digest  
   `466643afc3c393d49fcaddd4a92f5f8be63d64e88dc351c6c90921edc84a4454`,  
   identico al valore congelato in `tests/unit/services/rag/agentic/test_score_contract_crossing.py:487`. Il test confronta effettivamente i byte a `:527`. Il negativo modifica lo score a `:535` e richiede il messaggio specifico di mismatch a `:536`, generato da `services/rag/agentic/wa_package_builder.py:166–170`. Controprova indipendente: gli stessi byte alterati **vengono accettati con il loro digest corretto**. Il rifiuto non dipende da un errore estraneo.

5. **NOT-A-DEFECT — la proiezione `score_raw` è numericamente neutra.**  
   Il nuovo campo entra in un dizionario temporaneo passato allo scorer (`services/rag/agentic/wa_package_builder.py:603–614`). `calculate_evidence_score` legge soltanto `score` dai source, a `services/rag/agentic/reasoning_utils.py:610` e `:662`; nessun ramo legge `score_raw`. Quindi anche `None` è innocuo. Ho verificato **240 confronti equivalenti**, inclusi `None` e zero, senza differenze numeriche. Questa specifica cura non modifica chunk, hash o byte serviti. La PR complessiva aggiunge invece metadati ai nuovi pacchetti, cambiandone intenzionalmente la serializzazione; non va confusa con la sola proiezione dello scorer.

6. **NOT-A-DEFECT — 13c attraversa davvero il successo hybrid.**  
   I test esercitano il metodo di ricerca e il formatter reale, con il produttore simulato; non dimostrano da soli l’intera catena fino allo scorer. Eseguendo i loro corpi contro le funzioni reali, 13a/13b chiamano dense una volta; **13c chiama hybrid una volta e dense zero volte** (`tests/unit/services/rag/agentic/test_score_contract_crossing.py:619–632`). Ho inoltre forzato un’eccezione dentro il ramo hybrid: il fallback avviene e **13c fallisce**, perché produce `DENSE_FORMATTED`, incompatibile con l’asserzione a `:632`. Il fallback non può quindi mascherare il successo atteso.

7. **NOT-A-DEFECT — nessuna regressione delle superfici 1–7 già assolte.**  
   Ricontrollati: discriminante Qdrant (`core/qdrant_db.py:1338`); formula e boost invariati (`services/misc/result_formatter.py:90–106`); vocabolario validato (`core/score_provenance.py:132`); conservazione dello zero (`services/misc/result_formatter.py:136`); cache legacy dichiarata UNKNOWN (`services/search/search_service.py:1124`); trasporto del pacchetto come testo (`services/integrations/wa_broker.py:692`, `services/integrations/wa_codex_daemon.py:495`).
